# Skiri Pawnee root-first curriculum — lesson plans (with simplified pronunciation)

**Pronunciation key.** Each Pawnee form is followed by a respelling in ⟨ ⟩ using the project's English respelling (Parks Sound Key p. xvii; Blue Book pp. xvii–xxi): *ah ee oo* = long *aa ii uu*; *uh ih oo* = short *a i u*; *d* = Pawnee *r* (a quick tap, as in English 'ready'); *ts* = *c*; *'* = glottal stop; a syllable in CAPITALS carries Parks's accent mark. Parks's *c* is written *ch* before a vowel and *ts* elsewhere (Parks Rule 24). Respellings without ≈ come from Parks's own phonetic transcription of that headword (database column `simplified_pronunciation`, Phase 1.1c). Respellings marked ≈ are derived from the spelling alone, because the dictionary gives no phonetic transcription for inflected forms; they carry no stress information beyond written accents and are an aid, not an attestation. Blue Book forms are left in Blue Book spelling.

Generated by `scripts/build_root_curriculum.py` from `roots_ranked.json`, `affixes_ranked.json`, the project database (Parks dictionary), Parks Appendix 1, and the Blue Book (BB). Every Pawnee form below is looked up, not typed; each is followed by its source (Parks page and entry id, Appendix 1 page, or BB lesson and page). Orthography follows the source exactly: Parks forms use *ʔ*, *c*, long vowels *aa ii uu*; BB forms keep the BB spelling (*ts*, *'*, *•* between pieces).

Confidence labels come from the extraction: **high** = Parks's analysis reproduces the headword exactly or via a numbered sound rule; **medium** = reproduces it only with vowel-length / glottal tolerance, an unnumbered rule, or an inherited gloss; **low** = not reproducible, needs speaker/linguist review.

## How to read a lesson

Each lesson has: objectives; new roots or morphemes (with frequency rank and confidence); a construction walkthrough; attested example words (5–10); Practice A (build the word) and Practice B (break down the word); an answer key. Units end with a review built from attested words whose *every* morpheme has been taught.

# Unit 1 — People, kin and the creatures around us (roots as vocabulary)

Units 1–3 teach the highest-frequency roots as vocabulary. A root is shown with the simplest attested word that contains it, so learners hear a real word while meeting the piece that recurs in dozens of others. Suffixes that appear in these words (-uʔ, -kis) are only previewed here; they are taught in Unit 4.

**Blue Book alignment.** The Blue Book opens with short nouns (L1 p.29) and reaches people-words in L6 (p.55–56) and kinship in L7 (p.60–62). This unit pulls the person and kinship roots forward because they are the most frequent human-reference roots in the dictionary and because kinship words are the clearest case of roots that never stand alone.

## Lesson 1.1 — person, man, woman, child

**Objectives**

- Recognise the roots *icaahiks*, *wiita*, *icapaak*, *piira* inside attested words.
- Hear that a stem (the dictionary form) and the free noun can differ at the edges.
- Match each root to its Blue Book spelling.

**New roots** (type frequency = number of distinct dictionary words built on the root)

- **icaahiks** — 'person' — noun root (bound) — builds 24 dictionary words (rank 37) — confidence **medium** (root glossed consistently by Parks but only 20% of host words segment cleanly). Examples: *atiikicaahiks ⟨UH-tee-kih-chah-hihks⟩* 'Indian bean (generic) (Phaseolus spp.); ' (p.19); *icaahiks ⟨ih-chah-hihks⟩* 'be friendly, sociable; be affable.' (p.53); *icaahikskitawi ⟨ih-chah-hihks-kih-tuh-wih⟩* 'be a handsome person.' (p.53).
- **wiita** — 'male' — noun root (bound) — builds 10 dictionary words (rank 122) — confidence **medium** (root glossed consistently by Parks but only 0% of host words segment cleanly; 1 occurrence(s) inside Parks compound stems glossed by inheritance from the form's own entries). Examples: *piita ⟨PEE-tuh⟩* 'man; male.' (p.113); *Piitahaawiraata ⟨pee-tuh-hah-WIH-dah-tuh⟩* 'Pitahawirata band; (hist.) Tappage band.' (p.113); *cikirihpiita ⟨CHIH-kih-dihh-pee-tuh⟩* 'rooster, cock.' (p.29).
- **icapaak** — 'female' — noun root (bound) — builds 7 dictionary words (rank 166) — confidence **medium** (root glossed consistently by Parks but only 0% of host words segment cleanly). Examples: *capaaraʔu ⟨chuh-PAH-duh-'oo⟩* 'the wife (of him), his wife.' (p.28); *capaktihuks ⟨CHUH-puhk-tih-hooks⟩* 'widow.' (p.28); *cikirihcapaat ⟨CHIH-kih-dihh-chuh-paht⟩* 'hen.' (p.29).
- **piira** — 'child' — noun root (bound) — builds 14 dictionary words (rank 71) — confidence **high** (2 occurrence(s) inside Parks compound stems glossed by inheritance from the form's own entries). Examples: *piirakipiriiruʔ ⟨pee-duh-KIH-pih-dee-doo'⟩* 'young child, as a preteen.' (p.460); *piirakiripahki ⟨pee-duh-KIH-dih-puhh-kih⟩* 'baby, infant, as from birth to one to tw' (p.460); *piiraʔuʔ ⟨PEE-duh-'oo'⟩* 'baby, small child, child, as from a newb' (p.460).

**Construction walkthrough**

Parks lists the stem *caahiks ⟨CHAH-hihks⟩* under the root **icaahiks** (GO p.32 shows it incorporated in *tuuricaahiksii* 'he is a good-looking person'). The free noun *caahiks ⟨CHAH-hihks⟩* (human being; Parks p.26) lacks the initial *i-* of the stem; the sound-change engine does not model that loss, so the standalone word is flagged for review while compounds such as *piiriicaahiks ⟨pee-DEE-chah-hihks⟩* (Indian baby, Indian child) keep the full stem. **wiita** 'man, male' surfaces as *piita ⟨PEE-tuh⟩* (BB *pita* (L4)): *w* becomes *p* at the start of a word after the way Parks writes the stem — a *w ~ p* alternation the engine records as an unnumbered rule (medium). **icapaak** 'woman' gives *capaat ⟨CHUH-paht⟩* (final *k* → *t* word-finally, as Parks notes for stem-final *k*, GO p.46) and **piira** 'child' gives *piiraʔuʔ ⟨PEE-duh-'oo'⟩* with the noun suffix *-uʔ* (Unit 4). Compare the Blue Book list of people-words (BB p.55: “pita”; BB p.56: “tsuraki”).

**Blue Book cross-reference**

BB L6 p.55–56 lists *pita*, *tsapat*, *pirau'*, *piraski*, *tsuraki*, *tsurasu'*, *tsustit*. Parks spellings: *piita ⟨PEE-tuh⟩*, *capaat ⟨CHUH-paht⟩*, *piiraʔuʔ ⟨PEE-duh-'oo'⟩*, *piiraski ⟨PEE-duhs-kih⟩*, *cuuraki ⟨choo-duh-kih⟩*, *cuuraasuʔ ⟨choo-dah-soo'⟩*, *cuustit ⟨choos-tiht⟩*. BB *ts* = Parks *c*; BB does not mark vowel length consistently (BB *pita* vs Parks *piita*). ⚠ BB *pirau'* has no dictionary entry with that shape; the database holds it only as a Blue-Book-sourced entry (BB-pirau-0046).

**Practice and answer key**

**Practice A — build the word.** Combine the pieces (Parks's own analysis) into the attested word. Watch for the sound changes listed in the walkthrough.

1. *piira* 'child' + *uʔ* 'NOM -uʔ'  → ______ ('baby, small child, child, as from a newborn to age five')
2. *piira* 'child' + *icaahiks* 'human'  → ______ ('Indian baby, Indian child')
3. *cuura* 'female' + *kis* 'DIM -kis'  → ______ ('girl')
4. *icaahiks* 'person' + *kitawi* 'be leading'  → ______ ('be a handsome person')
5. *cikirir* 'chicken' + *wiita* 'male'  → ______ ('rooster, cock')

**Practice B — break down the word.** Divide each attested word into its morphemes and gloss each.

1. *piitakitawiʔuʔ ⟨PEE-tuh-kih-tuh-wih-'oo'⟩* 'leader, leading man'  → ______
2. *hariwiita ⟨huh-DIH-wee-tuh⟩* 'male calf'  → ______
3. *icapakitawi ⟨ih-chuh-puh-kih-tuh-wih⟩* 'be a beautiful woman'  → ______
4. *piirakiripahki ⟨pee-duh-KIH-dih-puhh-kih⟩* 'baby, infant, as from birth to one to two years of age'  → ______
5. *cuuraasuʔ ⟨choo-dah-soo'⟩* 'young unmarried woman, pubescent girl, maiden, virgin'  → ______

**Answer key (A and B)**

A1. *piiraʔuʔ ⟨PEE-duh-'oo'⟩* (Parks p.460, `SK-piiraquq-p460-4084`) — rules at the seams: ʔ-epenthesis (V+V) — segmentation confidence high
A2. *piiriicaahiks ⟨pee-DEE-chah-hihks⟩* (Parks p.460, `SK-piiriicaahiks-p460-4099`) — rules at the seams: 7 — segmentation confidence high
A3. *cuuraki ⟨choo-duh-kih⟩* (Parks p.32, `SK-cuuraki-p32-0536`; BB *tsuraki* (L6, p.56)) — rules at the seams: 9R — segmentation confidence high
A4. *icaahikskitawi ⟨ih-chah-hihks-kih-tuh-wih⟩* (Parks p.53, `SK-icaahikskitawi-p53-0908`) — segmentation confidence high
A5. *cikirihpiita ⟨CHIH-kih-dihh-pee-tuh⟩* (Parks p.29, `SK-cikirihpiita-p29-0443`) — rules at the seams: w>p — segmentation confidence medium
B1. *wiita* + *kitawi* + *uʔ* 'NOM -uʔ' (Parks p.113, `SK-piitakitawiquq-p113-2180`) — confidence medium
B2. *harii* 'calf' + *wiita* 'male' (Parks p.37, `SK-hariwiita-p37-0644`) — confidence medium
B3. *icapaak* + *kitawi* (Parks p.55, `SK-icapakitawi-p55-0928`) — confidence medium
B4. *piira* 'baby' + *kiripahkis* 'little' (Parks p.460, `SK-piirakiripahki-p460-4092`) — confidence high
B5. *cuuraas* 'pubescent girl' + *uʔ* 'NOM -uʔ' (Parks p.32, `SK-cuuraasuq-p32-0535`) — confidence high

**Where this deviates from the Blue Book, and why.** BB introduces these words in L6 after verbs; here they come first so that every later verb lesson can incorporate roots the learner already knows.

## Lesson 1.2 — kinship: stems that never stand alone

**Objectives**

- Learn the kinship stems (-aas- 'father', -saas-/-raa 'mother', -kaa- 'grandmother', -paak- 'grandfather', -raar- 'same-sex sibling', -waat- 'niece/nephew').
- Recognise the three possessor prefixes *ati-/t-* 'my', *a-* 'your', *i-* 'his/her' and the third-person ending *-riʔ/-tiʔ*.
- Compare Appendix 3, the dictionary entries and Blue Book L7 for the same terms.

**New roots** (type frequency = number of distinct dictionary words built on the root)

- **as** (father (kinship, atiʔas)) — 'father' — noun root (bound) — builds 2 dictionary words (rank 486) — confidence **high** (homonym split applied (see homonym_sense in root_extraction.py)). Examples: *atiʔas ⟨uh-TIH-'uhs⟩* 'my, our father.' (p.19); *aʔas ⟨UH-'uhs⟩* 'your father.' (p.349).
- **kaa** (grandmother (kinship, atikaʔ)) — 'grandmother' — noun root (bound) — builds 1 dictionary words (rank 950) — confidence **medium** (fewer than 3 direct Parks glosses and only 0% of host segmentations are high-confidence; attested in a single word only; homonym split applied (see homonym_sense in root_extraction.py)). Examples: *atikaʔ ⟨uh-TIH-kuh'⟩* 'my grandmother.' (p.19).
- **raar** (samesex sibling) — 'samesex sibling' — noun root (bound) — builds 2 dictionary words (rank 629) — confidence **high** (homonym split applied (see homonym_sense in root_extraction.py)). Examples: *iraariʔ ⟨ih-DAH-dih'⟩* 'his, their brother; her, their sister; m' (p.64); *ara ⟨UH-duh⟩* 'your same-sex sibling; i.e., your brothe' (p.360).

**New morphemes**

- KIN.POSS 1 *ati-* / *t-* 'my, our' — first-person possessor on kinship stems — source: Parks App.3; dictionary etymologies (e.g. ati+as, ati+kaa, (a)ti+waat); GO p.30 'kinship terms are uniquely inflected for inalienable possession'
- KIN.POSS 2 *a-* 'your' — second-person possessor — source: Parks App.3; etymologies a+as, a+kaa, a+raar
- KIN.POSS 3 *i-* (dictionary also *hi-*) 'his, her, their' + *-riʔ* — third-person possessor with a closing suffix — source: Parks App.3; etymologies i+raar+riʔ, i+saas+riʔ, hi-+aas+riʔ — ⚠ App.3 writes the father form with *i-*; the dictionary entry writes *hi-* (see review log).

**Construction walkthrough**

Parks (GO p.30) says kinship terms are the one noun class inflected for inalienable possession. The dictionary etymologies show the pattern: *atiʔas ⟨uh-TIH-'uhs⟩* = *ati* 'my' + *as* 'father' (p.19); *aʔas ⟨UH-'uhs⟩* = *a* 'your' + *as* (p.349, with ʔ inserted between the two vowels); *hiʔaastiʔ ⟨hih-'AHS-tih'⟩* = *hi-* + *aas* + *-riʔ* (p.40), where *s* + *r* gives *st* (Parks Rule 21, *r* → *t* after an obstruent). The same three-way pattern recurs: *atikaʔ ⟨uh-TIH-kuh'⟩* / *akaʔ ⟨UH-kuh'⟩* / *ikaariʔ ⟨ih-KAH-dih'⟩* 'grandmother'; *atipat ⟨uh-TIH-puht⟩* / *apat ⟨UH-puht⟩* / *ipaaktiʔ ⟨ih-PAHK-tih'⟩* 'grandfather' (*k* + *r* → *kt*, Rule 21); *iraariʔ ⟨ih-DAH-dih'⟩* for both 'my' and 'his/her' same-sex sibling, with *ara ⟨UH-duh⟩* 'your' (*a* + *raar*, final *r* lost, Rule 23). In *itaahiʔ ⟨ih-TAH-hih'⟩* 'his sister (of a male)' the stem *taat* meets *-riʔ*: *t* + *r* → *hr* → *h* (Rules 13, 14, 15). Appendix 3 table:

| Term (App.3) | Stem | my | your | his/her |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| sibling (same sex) | iraariʔ | iraariʔ | — | iraariʔ |
| mother | atiraʔ | atiraʔ | asaas | isaastiʔ |
| father | atiʔas | atiʔas | aʔas | iʔaastiʔ |
| grandmother | atikaʔ | atikaʔ | akaʔ | ikaariʔ |
| grandfather | atipaat | atipaat | apaat | ipaaktiʔ |
| male's nephew, niece | tiwaat | tiwaat | awaat | iwaahiʔ |
| mother's brother | -waciriks, -waariiriks | tiwaciriks | paciriks | iwariiriks |
| sister (of male) | -taat | itaahiʔ | ataati | itaahiʔ |
| sibling (of same sex as speaker) | -raar | iraariʔ | ara | iraariʔ |
| brother (of female) | -raac | iraactiʔ | araac | iraactiʔ |
| grandchild | -rak-tiikis | raktiiki | arakis | raktiiki |

Source: Parks Appendix 3 (kinship_paradigms table).

**Blue Book cross-reference**

BB L7 p.60–62 lists the same three sets (BB p.60: “atira'”; BB p.61: “asas”; BB p.62: “hidsti '	his father”). ⚠ Conflicts to show learners rather than hide: (1) 'my grandfather' — dictionary *atipat ⟨uh-TIH-puht⟩* (p.19) and BB *atipat*, but App.3 *atipaat*; (2) 'his father' — App.3 *iʔaastiʔ*, dictionary *hiʔaastiʔ ⟨hih-'AHS-tih'⟩*, BB *hiasti'* (OCR *hidsti'*); (3) 'your niece/nephew' — Parks *awaat ⟨UH-waht⟩* (p.20) but BB *swat* (p.61); (4) BB *tsuwat* 'my daughter' and *tiki'* 'my son' have no Parks kinship paradigm (*tiki ⟨TIH-kih⟩* is in the dictionary only as 'son, sonny', p.165).

**Practice and answer key**

**Practice A — build the word.** Combine the pieces (Parks's own analysis) into the attested word. Watch for the sound changes listed in the walkthrough.

**Answer key (A and B)**

A2. *akaʔ ⟨UH-kuh'⟩* (Parks p.9, `SK-akaq-p9-0103`; BB *aka'* (L7, p.61)) — segmentation confidence low
A3. *ara ⟨UH-duh⟩* (Parks p.360, `SK-ara-p360-3704`; BB *ara* (L7, p.61)) — rules at the seams: 23, tolerance: vowel length/ʔ/h — segmentation confidence medium
A4. *iraariʔ ⟨ih-DAH-dih'⟩* (Parks p.64, `SK-iraariq-p64-1121`; BB *irari '* (L6, p.56)) — rules at the seams: 20 — segmentation confidence high
A5. *isaastiʔ ⟨ih-SAHS-tih'⟩* (Parks p.68, `SK-isaastiq-p68-1213`) — rules at the seams: 21 — segmentation confidence high

B2. *ati* 'KIN.POSS 1 ati-/t-' + *kaa* 'grandmother' (Parks p.19, `SK-atikaq-p19-0233`) — confidence medium
B3. *i* 'KIN.POSS 3 i-' + *paak* 'grandfather' + *riʔ* 'KIN.3 -riʔ (third-person kinship suffix)' (Parks p.64, `SK-ipaaktiq-p64-1116`) — confidence high
B4. *i* 'KIN.POSS 3 i-' + *taat* 'sister (of a male)' + *riʔ* 'KIN.3 -riʔ (third-person kinship suffix)' (Parks p.68, `SK-itaahiq-p68-1216`) — confidence high
B5. *a* 'KIN.POSS 2 a-' + *raac* 'brother (of a female)' (Parks p.360, `SK-araac-p360-3706`) — confidence high

**Where this deviates from the Blue Book, and why.** BB teaches kinship in L7 after the verb phrase. It is moved here because the possessor prefixes are the simplest attested case of a prefix + root + suffix build, and they are needed to read BB dialogues from L6 on.

## Lesson 1.3 — animals, things, and the Blue Book's first nouns

**Objectives**

- Learn the roots *asaa* 'horse/dog', *raak* (variants *rak*, *haak*) 'wood', *hiit* 'feather', *akaar* 'dwelling', *rikiis* 'corn', *paapicis* 'metal'.
- Read the Blue Book L1 and L8 noun lists in Parks orthography.

**New roots** (type frequency = number of distinct dictionary words built on the root)

- **asaa** — 'horse' — noun root (bound) — builds 14 dictionary words (rank 67) — confidence **high**. Examples: *asaakaapiraat ⟨uh-sah-KAH-pih-daht⟩* 'rabid dog; rabid horse, as one bitten by' (p.362); *asaakaaruʔ ⟨UH-sah-kah-doo'⟩* 'barn.' (p.362); *asaakahuraaruʔ ⟨uh-sah-KUH-hoo-dah-doo'⟩* 'wild horse, mustang.' (p.362).
- **raak** — 'wood' — noun root (bound) — builds 80 dictionary words (rank 8) — confidence **medium** (root glossed consistently by Parks but only 24% of host words segment cleanly; surface variants merged: haak, rak). Examples: *arakis ⟨UH-duh-kihs⟩* 'sonny; daughter, an appelative for a you' (p.13); *rakis ⟨DUH-kihs⟩* 'wood; stick, limb, branch; pole.' (p.135); *raktahcuʔ ⟨DUHK-tuhh-choo'⟩* 'shield.' (p.135).
- **hiit** — 'feather' — N — builds 10 dictionary words (rank 109) — confidence **high** (1 occurrence(s) inside Parks compound stems glossed by inheritance from the form's own entries). Examples: *hiituʔ ⟨HEE-too'⟩* 'feather.' (p.390); *hiitaruhtuʔ ⟨HEE-tuh-dooh-too'⟩* 'soft feather, plume.' (p.390); *hiitkiisuʔ ⟨HEET-kee-soo'⟩* 'quill of a feather.' (p.390).
- **akaar** — 'dwelling' — N — builds 34 dictionary words (rank 24) — confidence **medium** (root glossed consistently by Parks but only 6% of host words segment cleanly; 1 occurrence(s) inside Parks compound stems glossed by inheritance from the form's own entries). Examples: *raarakaahki ⟨dah-DUH-kahh-kih⟩* 'tanned summer hide, as used for a tipi c' (p.122); *asaakaaruʔ ⟨UH-sah-kah-doo'⟩* 'barn.' (p.362); *akahwiriwisisuʔ ⟨UH-kuhh-wih-dih-wih-sih-soo'⟩* 'partition; partitioning wall.' (p.11).
- **rikiis** — 'corn' — N — builds 22 dictionary words (rank 44) — confidence **high**. Examples: *rikiisuʔ ⟨DIH-kee-soo'⟩* 'corn; corn kernel, shelled corn.' (p.492); *riikactikiisuʔ ⟨DEE-kuhts-tih-kee-soo'⟩* 'seed; seeds (generic).' (p.141); *tapahikiisuʔ ⟨TUH-puh-hih-kee-soo'⟩* 'g r o u n d  c o r n , cornmeal.' (p.158).
- **paapicis** — 'metal' — N — builds 14 dictionary words (rank 70) — confidence **high**. Examples: *paapicisuʔ ⟨PAH-pih-chih-soo'⟩* 'metal (generic); iron.' (p.454); *haspaapicisuʔ ⟨huhs-PAH-pih-chih-soo'⟩* 'chain; log chain.' (p.38); *haturaahpaapicisuʔ ⟨HUH-too-dahh-pah-pih-chih-soo'⟩* 'railroad (set of tracks) ; railroad trac' (p.387).

**Construction walkthrough**

**asaa** is a dependent stem (Parks GO p.29: *asaa-* 'horse; dog' cannot stand alone). With the diminutive it is *asaaki ⟨uh-SAH-kih⟩* 'dog' (p.362); the independent word for 'horse' is the unrelated *aruusaʔ ⟨uh-doo-suh'⟩* (p.362). **raak** 'wood' appears as *rak* in *rakis ⟨DUH-kihs⟩* (wood; BB *rakis* (L1, p.29)) and as *raak* in *raakuʔ ⟨DAH-koo'⟩* (box); Parks also writes *haak* for the same root (etymology of *raktiituʔ*: 'rak/haak'), so the extraction merges *rak* and *haak* under *raak* with medium confidence (vowel-length and *r ~ h* alternations are not numbered rules). **hiit** gives *hiituʔ ⟨HEE-too'⟩* (BB *hitu '* (L1, p.29)), **akaar** gives *akaaruʔ ⟨UH-kah-doo'⟩*, **rikiis** gives *rikiisuʔ ⟨DIH-kee-soo'⟩* (BB *rikisu'* (L11, p.79)), **paapicis** gives *paapicisuʔ ⟨PAH-pih-chih-soo'⟩* ('metal (generic)'; BB L8 p.68 *papitsisu'* 'metal, money').

# Unit 2 — The body and the mind

Body-part roots are the most productive noun roots in the dictionary and the nouns most often incorporated into verbs (Parks GO p.43). Each is shown with its free noun in *-uʔ* and with compounds.

**Blue Book alignment.** Follows the Blue Book's body lesson (L5, p.49–52) closely in vocabulary; the verb constructions of L5 are postponed to Unit 5.

## Lesson 2.1 — head, eye, mouth, hand, foot

**Objectives**

- Learn *paaks*, *kiriik*, *haaka*, *iks*, *as* and their free nouns.
- Notice vowel-length variants (*paaks ~ paks*, *kiriik ~ kirik*) that Parks writes in different words.

**New roots** (type frequency = number of distinct dictionary words built on the root)

- **paaks** — 'head' — noun root (bound) — builds 42 dictionary words (rank 19) — confidence **high** (surface variants merged: paks). Examples: *pakskuukuʔuʔ ⟨puhks-koo-koo-'oo'⟩* 'hat, cap.' (p.109); *paksuʔ ⟨PUHK-soo'⟩* 'head.' (p.110); *kucpaaksuʔ ⟨koots-pahk-soo'⟩* 'cast iron kettle.' (p.101).
- **kiriik** — 'eye' — N — builds 40 dictionary words (rank 20) — confidence **medium** (root glossed consistently by Parks but only 20% of host words segment cleanly; surface variants merged: kirik). Examples: *kiriikuʔ ⟨kih-DEE-koo'⟩* 'eye.' (p.95); *kirikaac ⟨KIH-dih-kahts⟩* 'cataract; gray eyed.' (p.96); *kirikaaʔat ⟨kih-dih-kah-'uht⟩* 'scrutinize, examine, study, look over ca' (p.96).
- **haaka** — 'mouth' — noun root (bound) — builds 25 dictionary words (rank 34) — confidence **high** (2 occurrence(s) inside Parks compound stems glossed by inheritance from the form's own entries). Examples: *haakaawatatuuk ⟨hah-kah-wuh-tuh-too(k/t)⟩* 'open, take a lid off, as a box or can; t' (p.33); *haakaʔuʔ ⟨HAH-kuh-'oo'⟩* 'mouth.' (p.33); *haakakiraaruʔ ⟨hah-KUH-kih-dah-doo'⟩* 'saliva; drool, drivel.' (p.34).
- **iks** — 'hand' — N — builds 51 dictionary words (rank 15) — confidence **medium** (root glossed consistently by Parks but only 27% of host words segment cleanly; 1 occurrence(s) inside Parks compound stems glossed by inheritance from the form's own entries). Examples: *iksuʔ ⟨IHK-soo'⟩* 'hand.' (p.64); *iksiitik ⟨ihk-see-tih(k/t)⟩* 'wave, wave the hand in a dropping motion' (p.63); *ikskaa ⟨ihks-kah⟩* 'have one's hand in, as a glove or drawer' (p.63).
- **as** — 'foot' — N — builds 53 dictionary words (rank 14) — confidence **medium** (root glossed consistently by Parks but only 25% of host words segment cleanly; 13 occurrence(s) inside Parks compound stems glossed by inheritance from the form's own entries). Examples: *asuʔ ⟨UH-soo'⟩* 'foot; paw.' (p.18); *kaaʔasasuʔ ⟨KAH-'uh-suh-soo' / KAH-suh-soo'⟩* 'car wheel, automobile wheel.' (p.2); *askakiiriituʔ ⟨UHS-kuh-kee-dee-too'⟩* 'heel (of foot).' (p.16).

**Construction walkthrough**

Parks's own Table 5 example is *iksuʔ ⟨IHK-soo'⟩* 'hand' < *iks-* + *-uʔ* (GO p.31). The same build gives *paksuʔ ⟨PUHK-soo'⟩*, *kiriikuʔ ⟨kih-DEE-koo'⟩*, *asuʔ ⟨UH-soo'⟩* and *haakaʔuʔ ⟨HAH-kuh-'oo'⟩*; in the last, the stem ends in a vowel, so a glottal stop separates it from *-uʔ* (the engine logs this as ʔ-epenthesis). The root for 'head' is *paaks* in *pakskiraaruʔ ⟨PUHKS-kih-dah-doo'⟩* ('brains') but short *paks* in *paksuʔ ⟨PUHK-soo'⟩* and *pakskuukuʔuʔ ⟨puhks-koo-koo-'oo'⟩*; 'eye' is *kiriik* in *kiriikuʔ ⟨kih-DEE-koo'⟩* but *kirik* in *kiriktaakuʔ ⟨KIH-dihk-tah-koo'⟩*. These length alternations are merged as variants of one root (medium confidence; no numbered rule).

## Lesson 2.2 — bone, face, nose, neck, hair, leg, belly, tongue, arm

**Objectives**

- Learn *kiis*, *icka*, *icus*, *kiic*, *uus*, *kaas*, *karaar*, *hat*, *wiir*.
- See a second case of stem-initial *i-* dropping in free nouns (*icka-* → *ckaʔuʔ*, *icus-* → *cusuʔ*).

**New roots** (type frequency = number of distinct dictionary words built on the root)

- **kiis** — 'bone' — N — builds 48 dictionary words (rank 17) — confidence **high** (8 occurrence(s) inside Parks compound stems glossed by inheritance from the form's own entries). Examples: *Kiisaakaʔuʔ ⟨kee-SAH-kuh-'oo'⟩* '(formerly) Scalp Dance; (contemp.) Victo' (p.91); *kiisawataar ⟨kee-suh-wuh-tah(d)⟩* 'be pure white, bright white.' (p.92); *kiiskaac ⟨kees-kahts⟩* 'be pure white.' (p.92).
- **icka** — 'face' — incorporated element in verbs — builds 33 dictionary words (rank 26) — confidence **high** (5 occurrence(s) inside Parks compound stems glossed by inheritance from the form's own entries). Examples: *ickaʔat ⟨ihts-kuh-'uht⟩* 'be the end, extremity, as of an object .' (p.56); *ickaʔawask ⟨ihts-kuh-'uh-wuhs(k)⟩* 'smile.' (p.56); *ickahak ⟨ihts-kuh-huh(k/t)⟩* 'be the end, extremity, as of an object o' (p.57).
- **icus** — 'nose' — noun root (bound) — builds 19 dictionary words (rank 52) — confidence **high**. Examples: *icusaa ⟨ih-choo-sah⟩* 'snort, as an animal.' (p.59); *icusawask ⟨ih-choo-suh-wuhs(k)⟩* 'smile, grin, as when laughing to oneself' (p.59); *icuskatawii ⟨ih-choos-kuh-tuh-wee⟩* 'be pug-nosed, have a pug nose; have a sm' (p.59).
- **kiic** — 'neck' — N — builds 21 dictionary words (rank 46) — confidence **high** (2 occurrence(s) inside Parks compound stems glossed by inheritance from the form's own entries). Examples: *haskiicuʔ ⟨HUHS-kee-choo'⟩* 'string, cord, twine, thong , as used for' (p.37); *ikskiicuʔ ⟨ihks-KEE-choo'⟩* 'finger.' (p.63); *kiica ⟨kee-chuh⟩* 'have, be wearing around one's neck, as a' (p.89).
- **uus** — 'hair' — N — builds 10 dictionary words (rank 120) — confidence **high**. Examples: *uusuʔ ⟨oo-soo'⟩* 'hair.' (p.173); *uusaakuʔ ⟨oo-sah-koo'⟩* 'box-elder (Acer negundo).' (p.173); *uuskaraaruʔ ⟨oos-kuh-dah-doo'⟩* 'hairline; hair on the head.' (p.173).
- **kaas** — 'leg' — N — builds 10 dictionary words (rank 111) — confidence **high**. Examples: *kaasik ⟨kah-sih(k/t)⟩* 'break, crack, as a nut shell; split, bre' (p.73); *kaastakis ⟨KAHS-tuh-kihs⟩* 'artificial leg.' (p.74); *kaasuʔ ⟨KAH-soo'⟩* 'leg.' (p.74).
- **karaar** — 'belly' — N — builds 10 dictionary words (rank 112) — confidence **high**. Examples: *karaaricakus ⟨kuh-dah-dih-chuh-koo(s)⟩* 'be fat, corpulent, relatively large in t' (p.77); *karaarukuk ⟨kuh-dah-doo-koo(k/t)⟩* 'belch, burp.' (p.77); *karaaruʔ ⟨kuh-DAH-doo'⟩* 'belly, abdomen; stomach.' (p.77).
- **hat** — 'tongue' — noun root (bound) — builds 9 dictionary words (rank 126) — confidence **medium** (root glossed consistently by Parks but only 22% of host words segment cleanly; 3 occurrence(s) inside Parks compound stems glossed by inheritance from the form's own entries). Examples: *hatkatakusiriʔ ⟨HUHT-kuh-tuh-koo-sih-dih'⟩* 'roof of the mouth; palatal, velar, and u' (p.38); *hatkiraar ⟨huht-kih-dah(d)⟩* 'stutter, as when one cannot speak plainl' (p.387); *haatuʔ ⟨HAH-too'⟩* 'tongue.' (p.36).
- **wiir** — 'arm' — noun root (bound) — builds 15 dictionary words (rank 66) — confidence **medium** (root glossed consistently by Parks but only 7% of host words segment cleanly; surface variants merged: piir; 2 occurrence(s) inside Parks compound stems glossed by inheritance from the form's own entries). Examples: *rahkasiipiiruʔ ⟨duhh-KUH-see-pee-doo'⟩* 'sleeve, garment sleeve.' (p.131); *katawiir ⟨kuh-tuh-wee(d)⟩* 'be leaning against (a vertical surface).' (p.82); *kicpiir ⟨kihts-pee(d)⟩* 'be a sprinkle, light shower; be misty.' (p.87).

**Construction walkthrough**

*kiisuʔ ⟨KEE-soo'⟩*, *kiicuʔ ⟨KEE-choo'⟩*, *uusuʔ ⟨oo-soo'⟩*, *kaasuʔ ⟨KAH-soo'⟩*, *karaaruʔ ⟨kuh-DAH-doo'⟩* are plain root + *-uʔ*. *ckaʔuʔ ⟨TSKUH-'oo'⟩* 'face' and *cusuʔ ⟨choo-soo'⟩* 'nose' drop the *i-* that the stems *icka-* and *icus-* keep inside verbs (*ickaʔat ⟨ihts-kuh-'uht⟩*, Unit 3); Parks gives the etymology *icka+uʔ*, so the free noun is flagged medium/low. *haatuʔ ⟨HAH-too'⟩* 'tongue' lengthens *hat* to *haat* before *-uʔ* (vowel-length alternation, medium). *piiruʔ ⟨PEE-doo'⟩* 'arm' shows *wiir* → *piir*, the same *w ~ p* alternation as *wiita* → *piita* (BB L3 p.41 states it as 'w changes to p after a hard consonant'; Parks has no numbered rule for the word-initial case — flagged). Compounds: *ikskiicuʔ ⟨ihks-KEE-choo'⟩* 'finger' = *iks* + *kiic* + *uʔ* (hand-neck); *haskiicuʔ ⟨HUHS-kee-choo'⟩* = *has* 'string' + *kiic* + *uʔ*; *kaastakis ⟨KAHS-tuh-kihs⟩* 'artificial leg' = *kaas* + *rakis* with *r* → *t* after *s* (Rule 21).

## Lesson 2.3 — mind, speech, image: roots for inner life

**Objectives**

- Learn *aciks* 'thought, mind' (also 'trachea'), *wakur* 'speech, voice', *awi* 'image'.
- See roots that live almost only inside verbs.

**New roots** (type frequency = number of distinct dictionary words built on the root)

- **aciks** — 'thought' — VD — builds 83 dictionary words (rank 7) — confidence **medium** (gloss disagreement across entries (top gloss share 46%) — possible homonymy/polysemy; needs linguist review; 13 occurrence(s) inside Parks compound stems glossed by inheritance from the form's own entries). Examples: *acikstaahiis ⟨uh-chihks-tah-hees⟩* 'throat to be dry; be thirsty.' (p.7); *acikstahuruu ⟨uh-chihks-tuh-hoo-doo⟩* 'convince, persuade' (p.7); *acikstaʔuk ⟨uh-chihks-tuh-'oo(k/t)⟩* 'decide, make up one's mind; convince or ' (p.7).
- **wakur** — 'speech' — incorporated element in verbs — builds 20 dictionary words (rank 48) — confidence **high**. Examples: *wakuhka ⟨wuh-kooh-kuh⟩* 'praise, single out verbally for praise o' (p.176); *wakuhkiisik ⟨wuh-kooh-kee-sih(k/t)⟩* 'brag, boast.' (p.176); *wakuhkiriwis ⟨wuh-kooh-kih-dih-wihs⟩* 'be devious, deceptive in one's speech.' (p.176).
- **awi** — 'image' — incorporated element in verbs — builds 91 dictionary words (rank 6) — confidence **medium** (root glossed consistently by Parks but only 18% of host words segment cleanly; 25 occurrence(s) inside Parks compound stems glossed by inheritance from the form's own entries). Examples: *awiʔaar ⟨uh-wih-'ah(d)⟩* 'turn into, transform into.' (p.21); *awiʔat ⟨uh-wih-'uht⟩* 'jump.' (p.21); *awiʔiik ⟨uh-wih-'ee(k/t)⟩* 'be tired, exhausted.' (p.21).

**Construction walkthrough**

**aciks** builds 83 dictionary words: as a descriptive verb *aciks ⟨uh-chihks⟩* 'have a thought' and as the first element of *acikstaʔuk ⟨uh-chihks-tuh-'oo(k/t)⟩* 'decide, make up one's mind', *acikstiihuur ⟨uh-chihks-tee-hoo(d)⟩* 'be thankful, appreciative, grateful', *aciksicaahiks ⟨uh-chihk-sih-chah-hihks⟩* 'be embarrassed, chagrined' (two roots the learner knows: thought + person). In *acikcak ⟨uh-chihk-chuh(k/t)⟩* 'cough' the final *s* of *aciks* drops before *cak* (Rule 9R, final-*s* loss). Parks glosses the same root 'trachea' in *acikstaahiis ⟨uh-chihks-tah-hees⟩* and the free noun *ciksuʔ ⟨CHIHK-soo'⟩* is 'esophagus, gullet' — one root covering the windpipe and the thoughts; the extraction keeps it as one root and flags the gloss spread. **wakur** 'speech' loses its final *r* before a consonant (*wakuhka ⟨wuh-kooh-kuh⟩*, *wakuhkiisik ⟨wuh-kooh-kee-sih(k/t)⟩*; Rule 23 as logged) and keeps it before a vowel (*wakuraah ⟨wuh-koo-dah(h)⟩*). **awi** 'image' gives *awiʔuʔ ⟨UH-wih-'oo'⟩* 'picture' (BB L1 p.29 hawiu) and the verb *awiʔat ⟨uh-wih-'uht⟩* 'jump'.

**Attested example words**

| **acikstaʔuk ⟨uh-chihks-tuh-'oo(k/t)⟩** | VR (3) | decide, make up one's mind | aciks 'thoughts' + raʔuk 'CAUS -raʔuk' | Parks p.7, `SK-acikstaquk-p7-0049` |
| **acikstiihuur ⟨uh-chihks-tee-hoo(d)⟩** | VD | be thankful, appreciative, grateful | aciks 'thoughts' + tiihuur 'to be special, i.e., charitable' | Parks p.7, `SK-acikstiihuur-p7-0064` |
| **acikcak ⟨uh-chihk-chuh(k/t)⟩** | VP (4) | cough | aciks 'trachea' + cak 'strike the' | Parks p.351, `SK-acikcak-p351-3613` |
| **aciksicaahiks ⟨uh-chihk-sih-chah-hihks⟩** | VD | be embarrassed, chagrined | aciks + icaahiks | Parks p.352, `SK-aciksicaahiks-p352-3624` |
| **wakuraah ⟨wuh-koo-dah(h)⟩** | VT (1) | agree to, comply with | wakur 'someone's words' + raah 'have/carry' | Parks p.176, `SK-wakuraah-p176-3488` |
| **wakuhka ⟨wuh-kooh-kuh⟩** | VT (4) | praise, single out verbally for praise or prominence | wakur 'speech' + ka 'to be outstanding' | Parks p.176, `SK-wakuhka-p176-3480` |

| **awiʔat ⟨uh-wih-'uht⟩** | VI (1) | jump | awi 'image' + at 'to go' | Parks p.21, `SK-awiqat-p21-0283` |

**Practice and answer key**

Answer key (Parks's analysis, sound rules fired, confidence):

- *ickakaa ⟨ihts-kuh-kah⟩* = icka 'face' + kaa 'to be inside' — no change at the seams — high (Parks p.57, `SK-ickakaa-p57-0978`)
- *ikskaa ⟨ihks-kah⟩* = iks 'hand' + kaa 'to be in' — no change at the seams — high (Parks p.63, `SK-ikskaa-p63-1087`)

# Unit 3 — Movement, position, everyday actions, and qualities

The verb roots that build the most stems: Parks notes that *at* 'go' and *hak* 'pass' each have hundreds of derived stems (GO p.31). Position roots (*kus*, *sa*, *arik*) and the locative stem elements *kaa*, *kata*, *kita*, *huka* are learned together because they almost always occur together. Verb forms shown here are the dictionary's own cited forms; how the prefixes work is the subject of Unit 5.

**Blue Book alignment.** BB meets these verbs in L5–L6 (*ti•wari'*, *ti•kikat*, *tut•ari'*, *ti•ruku'*) and L8 (*t'erit*, *ti•kaku'*). The colour and size roots are BB L2–L3.

## Lesson 3.1 — going and coming

**Objectives**

- Learn *at* 'go' (rank 1), *a (ir...)* 'come', *hak* 'pass', *wic* 'arrive', *warii* 'go about', and the plural/dual stems of 'go'.
- Read the irregular-root table (Parks Appendix 2).

**New roots** (type frequency = number of distinct dictionary words built on the root)

- **at** — 'go' — VI (1) — builds 156 dictionary words (rank 1) — confidence **medium** (root glossed consistently by Parks but only 23% of host words segment cleanly; 65 occurrence(s) inside Parks compound stems glossed by inheritance from the form's own entries). Examples: *tuksat ⟨took-suht⟩* 'covet, desire, want to have; ref. to a m' (p.166); *kaaʔat ⟨kah-'uht⟩* 'go, walk inside, as in a dwelling or roo' (p.2); *kaaʔiisat ⟨kah-'ee-suht⟩* 'go home; go, return to one's village.' (p.2).
- **a** — 'come' — verb root (bound), with preverb *ir-* — builds 113 dictionary words (rank 4) — confidence **medium** (root glossed consistently by Parks but only 25% of host words segment cleanly; 53 occurrence(s) inside Parks compound stems glossed by inheritance from the form's own entries). Examples: *aki ⟨uh-KIH⟩* 'and here.' (p.11); *raʔa ⟨dah⟩* 'bring.' (p.114); *kaaʔa ⟨kah-'uh⟩* 'come through; sprout, come up, as a plan' (p.2).
- **hak** — 'pass' — verb root (bound) — builds 69 dictionary words (rank 10) — confidence **medium** (root glossed consistently by Parks but only 16% of host words segment cleanly; 45 occurrence(s) inside Parks compound stems glossed by inheritance from the form's own entries). Examples: *kaahak ⟨kah-huh(k/t)⟩* 'walk around, pass around inside an enclo' (p.2); *ickahak ⟨ihts-kuh-huh(k/t)⟩* 'be the end, extremity, as of an object o' (p.57); *iriwiraahak ⟨ih-dih-wih-dah-huh(k/t)⟩* 'fall, tip over sideways; tip over to one' (p.68).
- **wic** — 'arrive' — incorporated element in verbs — builds 16 dictionary words (rank 60) — confidence **high** (6 occurrence(s) inside Parks compound stems glossed by inheritance from the form's own entries). Examples: *huutuhwica ⟨hoo-tooh-wih-chuh⟩* 'wind to come up, to begin blowing; wind ' (p.52); *kusihwica ⟨koo-sihh-wih-chuh⟩* 'arrive running (coming); reach a point r' (p.103); *kusihwicat ⟨koo-sihh-wih-chuht⟩* 'arrive running (going); reach a point ru' (p.103).
- **warii** — 'around in a line' — VI (4) — builds 20 dictionary words (rank 49) — confidence **medium** (root glossed consistently by Parks but only 18% of host words segment cleanly; 10 occurrence(s) inside Parks compound stems glossed by inheritance from the form's own entries). Examples: *awarii ⟨uh-wuh-dee⟩* 'fly around , as a bird .' (p.21); *ruuwarii ⟨doo-wuh-dee⟩* 'carry around; go around with.' (p.150); *warii ⟨wuh-dee⟩* 'go around, walk around; go about, walk a' (p.177).
- **wuu** — 'going in a line' — VI (4) — builds 16 dictionary words (rank 61) — confidence **medium** (root glossed consistently by Parks but only 0% of host words segment cleanly; 10 occurrence(s) inside Parks compound stems glossed by inheritance from the form's own entries). Examples: *Piiraʔuʔ Rarahkaataʔiispuu ⟨PEE-duh-'oo'duh-duhh-kah-tuh-'ees-poo⟩* 'September.' (p.460); *awuu ⟨uh-woo⟩* 'throw a spear, as in a game.' (p.26); *hawiriwus ⟨huh-WIH-dih-woos⟩* 'seine; net, fishnet.' (p.40).

**Construction walkthrough**

*at* stands alone as the verb *at ⟨uht⟩* 'go' (3sg *tiʔat ⟨≈tih-'uht⟩*, p.18) and ends hundreds of stems: *kaaʔat ⟨kah-'uht⟩* 'go, walk inside, as in a dwelling or room' (*kaa* 'inside' + *at*, with ʔ between the vowels), *ickaʔat ⟨ihts-kuh-'uht⟩*, *kitaʔat ⟨kih-tuh-'uht⟩*, *awiʔat ⟨uh-wih-'uht⟩* 'jump', *wicat ⟨wih-chuht⟩* 'arrive (going)'. *a* 'come' is a discontinuous stem with the preverb *ir-* (Parks GO p.31–32: 'the root a has no independent meaning of come' outside this stem); its 3sg is *taaʔ ⟨≈tah'⟩* and with *kaa* it is *kaaʔa ⟨kah-'uh⟩* 'come through' (3sg *takaaʔaʔ ⟨≈tuh-kah-'uh'⟩*). *hak* 'pass' gives *kaahak ⟨kah-huh(k/t)⟩*, *katahak ⟨kuh-tuh-huh(k/t)⟩*, *ickahak ⟨ihts-kuh-huh(k/t)⟩*. 'Go' is irregular in number: singular *at*, dual *war*, plural *wuu* (Appendix 2, irregular_verb_roots), seen in App.1 *tiʔat ⟨≈tih-'uht⟩* / *sitiwa ⟨≈sih-tih-wuh⟩* / *tiiwuuʔ, tiraarat ⟨≈tee-woo' / tih-dah-duht⟩*; the Blue Book states the same alternation (BB p.54: “for different subjects. Notice the roots for go are +at for one”).

**Practice and answer key**

**Practice A — build the word.** Combine the pieces (Parks's own analysis) into the attested word. Watch for the sound changes listed in the walkthrough.

1. *kaa* 'inside' + *at* 'go'  → ______ ('go, walk inside, as in a dwelling or room')
2. *wic* 'to a point' + *at* 'go'  → ______ ('arrive (going)')
3. *a* 'go' + *warii* 'around in a line'  → ______ ('fly around , as a bird ')
4. *icka* 'end' + *hak* 'to pass by'  → ______ ('be the end, extremity, as of an object or line ')
5. *kita* 'on top' + *at* 'go'  → ______ ('go, walk on top')

**Practice B — break down the word.** Divide each attested word into its morphemes and gloss each.

1. *haaʔat ⟨hah-'uht⟩* 'wade across water'  → ______
2. *awiʔat ⟨uh-wih-'uht⟩* 'jump'  → ______
3. *cirasat ⟨chih-duh-suht⟩* 'take, lead (going)'  → ______
4. *katahak ⟨kuh-tuh-huh(k/t)⟩* 'move back, move away, get back, back away, as from an object or a crowd'  → ______
5. *huukitahaawarii ⟨hoo-kih-tuh-hah-wuh-dee⟩* 'ice-skate, skate on ice'  → ______

**Answer key (A and B)**

A1. *kaaʔat ⟨kah-'uht⟩* (Parks p.2, `SK-kaaqat-p2-0010`) — rules at the seams: ʔ-epenthesis (V+V) — segmentation confidence high
A2. *wicat ⟨wih-chuht⟩* (Parks p.178, `SK-wicat-p178-3529`) — segmentation confidence high
A3. *awarii ⟨uh-wuh-dee⟩* (Parks p.21, `SK-awarii-p21-0272`) — segmentation confidence high
A4. *ickahak ⟨ihts-kuh-huh(k/t)⟩* (Parks p.57, `SK-ickahak-p57-0975`) — segmentation confidence high
A5. *kitaʔat ⟨kih-tuh-'uht⟩* (Parks p.98, `SK-kitaqat-p98-1907`) — rules at the seams: ʔ-epenthesis (V+V) — segmentation confidence high
B1. *haa* 'in water' + *at* 'go' (Parks p.33, `SK-haaqat-p33-0548`) — confidence high
B2. *awi* 'image' + *at* 'to go' (Parks p.21, `SK-awiqat-p21-0283`) — confidence high
B3. *ciras* 'COM ciras-' + *at* 'go' (Parks p.29, `SK-cirasat-p29-0451`) — confidence high
B4. *kata* '(away from) a vertical surface' + *hak* 'pass' (Parks p.80, `SK-katahak-p80-1480`) — confidence high
B5. *huukita* 'on top of' + *haa* 'water' + *warii* 'go about' (Parks p.398, `SK-huukitahaawarii-p398-3883`) — confidence high

## Lesson 3.2 — sitting, lying, standing — and where

**Objectives**

- Learn the position roots *kus* 'be sitting', *sa* 'be lying', *arik* 'be standing', *wiitik* 'sit down'.
- Learn the locative stem elements *kaa* 'inside', *kata* 'against a vertical surface', *kita* 'on top', *huka* 'in a shallow space' and combine them with the position roots.

**New roots** (type frequency = number of distinct dictionary words built on the root)

- **kus** — 'sitting' — VI (4) — builds 96 dictionary words (rank 5) — confidence **high** (24 occurrence(s) inside Parks compound stems glossed by inheritance from the form's own entries). Examples: *awatatakus ⟨uh-wuh-tuh-tuh-koo(s)⟩* 'be sitting on, on top; be piled, piled u' (p.21); *awiirakus ⟨uh-wee-duh-koo(s)⟩* 'be sitting in the back, be sitting in th' (p.23); *ciraskus ⟨chih-duhs-koo(s)⟩* 'live with, live together with, have livi' (p.29).
- **sa** — 'lying' — VI (4) — builds 25 dictionary words (rank 36) — confidence **medium** (root glossed consistently by Parks but only 28% of host words segment cleanly; 6 occurrence(s) inside Parks compound stems glossed by inheritance from the form's own entries). Examples: *awiirasa ⟨uh-wee-duh-suh⟩* 'be lying in back of, be lying beside.' (p.23); *caakasa ⟨chah-kuh-suh⟩* 'be a bridge, as over a river, creek, dit' (p.26); *hasa ⟨huh-suh⟩* 'be lying in water.' (p.37).
- **arik** (be standing) — 'standing' — VI (2-i) — builds 23 dictionary words (rank 40) — confidence **high** (5 occurrence(s) inside Parks compound stems glossed by inheritance from the form's own entries; homonym split applied (see homonym_sense in root_extraction.py)). Examples: *arikiihk ⟨uh-dih-kee(h)(k/t)⟩* 'stand, place standing, set standing.' (p.13); *awiiraarik ⟨uh-wee-dah-dih(k/t)⟩* 'be standing behind, standing in back of.' (p.23); *hukaarik ⟨hoo-kah-dih(k/t)⟩* 'be standing in, as in a vehicle or conve' (p.44).
- **wiitik** — 'sit down' — VI (4) — builds 18 dictionary words (rank 58) — confidence **high** (3 occurrence(s) inside Parks compound stems glossed by inheritance from the form's own entries). Examples: *hawiitik ⟨huh-wee-tih(k/t)⟩* 'sit down in water.' (p.40); *itkahaariwiitik ⟨iht-kuh-hah-dih-wee-tih(k/t)⟩* 'listen; overhear, as when one accidental' (p.69); *ituhwiitik ⟨ih-tooh-wee-tih(k/t)⟩* '(pl. subj.) set up a village; make a vil' (p.69).
- **kaa** — 'inside' — VL — builds 55 dictionary words (rank 13) — confidence **high** (23 occurrence(s) inside Parks compound stems glossed by inheritance from the form's own entries). Examples: *kaahak ⟨kah-huh(k/t)⟩* 'walk around, pass around inside an enclo' (p.2); *kaaʔa ⟨kah-'uh⟩* 'come through; sprout, come up, as a plan' (p.2); *kaaʔaah ⟨kah-'ah(h)⟩* 'be inside, as in a dwelling.' (p.2).
- **kata** — 'against a vertical surface' — incorporated element in verbs — builds 76 dictionary words (rank 9) — confidence **medium** (root glossed consistently by Parks but only 11% of host words segment cleanly; 37 occurrence(s) inside Parks compound stems glossed by inheritance from the form's own entries). Examples: *katahak ⟨kuh-tuh-huh(k/t)⟩* 'move back, move away, get back, back awa' (p.80); *katahiis ⟨kuh-tuh-hees⟩* 'be dull, blunt.' (p.80); *kataʔuukuk ⟨kuh-tuh-'oo-koo(k/t)⟩* 'bump against; get up against, as a verti' (p.80).
- **kita** — 'on top' — noun root (bound) — builds 26 dictionary words (rank 33) — confidence **high** (10 occurrence(s) inside Parks compound stems glossed by inheritance from the form's own entries). Examples: *kickita ⟨kihts-kih-tuh⟩* 'be a branch, fork in a stream, as a cree' (p.87); *kitahaaruʔ ⟨KIH-tuh-hah-doo'⟩* 'hilltop, top of a hill.' (p.98); *kitahiis ⟨kih-tuh-hees⟩* 'be dull, blunt .' (p.98).
- **huka** — 'in a shallow circumscribed space' — VL — builds 18 dictionary words (rank 54) — confidence **high** (6 occurrence(s) inside Parks compound stems glossed by inheritance from the form's own entries). Examples: *hukaʔiituʔ ⟨hoo-kuh-'ee-too'⟩* '(formerly) wagon box; (contemp.) truck b' (p.43); *hukaarik ⟨hoo-kah-dih(k/t)⟩* 'be standing in, as in a vehicle or conve' (p.44); *hukahaaruʔ ⟨hoo-kuh-hah-doo'⟩* 'bottom land, bottoms of a stream valley,' (p.44).

**Construction walkthrough**

Position roots stand alone (*kus ⟨koo(s)⟩* 3sg *tiiku ⟨≈tee-koo⟩*; *sa ⟨suh⟩* 3sg *tiisa ⟨≈tee-suh⟩*; *arik ⟨uh-dih(k/t)⟩* 3sg *tiirit ⟨≈tee-diht⟩*; *wiitik ⟨wee-tih(k/t)⟩* 3sg *tiwiitit ⟨≈tih-wee-tiht⟩*) and take a locative element in front (Parks Table 15, GO p.44: locative slot before the root): *kaakus ⟨kah-koo(s)⟩* 'be inside , as a dwelling ', *kaasa ⟨kah-suh⟩* 'be lying inside, as in a room ', *kaarik ⟨kah-dih(k/t)⟩* 'be standing there' (Parks writes short *ka* here), *kitakus ⟨kih-tuh-koo(s)⟩* 'be sitting on top', *hukakus ⟨hoo-kuh-koo(s)⟩* 'be sitting in, as a vehicle', *katasa ⟨kuh-tuh-suh⟩* 'be on the side of, as against the surface of an object'. The elements are themselves locative verbs: *kaa ⟨kah⟩* 'be inside, as in a dwelling or enclosed space' (3sg *tikaa ⟨≈tih-kah⟩*), *huka ⟨hoo-kuh⟩* (3sg *tihuka ⟨≈tih-hoo-kuh⟩*). Note the attested shapes: *arik ⟨uh-dih(k/t)⟩* ends in *k* but its 3sg is *tiirit ⟨≈tee-diht⟩* (*k* → *t* word-finally; i + a → ii, Rule 7); *wiitik ⟨wee-tih(k/t)⟩* 3sg *tiwiitit ⟨≈tih-wee-tiht⟩* likewise.

## Lesson 3.3 — everyday actions

**Objectives**

- Learn *raʔuk* 'make', *raah* 'have, carry', *warik* 'throw; cause to go', *wuh* 'put', *cak* 'strike', *aar* 'become / do', *kuut* 'die', *iirik* 'see', *kiikaa* 'drink', *kikak* 'cry'.
- Notice that some roots are rare in the dictionary's etymologies but central in speech (*kikak*, *kiikaa*).

**New roots** (type frequency = number of distinct dictionary words built on the root)

- **raʔuk** — 'causative' — verb root (bound) — builds 12 dictionary words (rank 91) — confidence **medium** (gloss disagreement across entries (top gloss share 17%) — possible homonymy/polysemy; needs linguist review). Examples: *kituhaʔuk ⟨kih-too-huh-'oo(k/t)⟩* 'clear one's throat.' (p.99); *taraʔuk ⟨tuh-duh-'oo(k/t)⟩* 'pick, pick off, pluck' (p.159); *hiikaraʔuk ⟨hee-kuh-duh-'oo(k/t)⟩* 'wash, wash clean, clean by washing.' (p.389).
- **raah** — 'have' — VT (1) — builds 18 dictionary words (rank 56) — confidence **medium** (gloss disagreement across entries (top gloss share 46%) — possible homonymy/polysemy; needs linguist review). Examples: *aktakuraah ⟨uhk-tuh-koo-dah(h)⟩* 'be a couple; be a married couple; be mar' (p.11); *hukataahpiiraah ⟨hoo-kuh-tahh-pee-dah(h)⟩* 'carry in one's arms, as when having arms' (p.44); *ikstaah ⟨ihks-tah(h)⟩* 'have, carry, hold in one's hand.' (p.63).
- **warik** — 'cause to' — verb root (bound) — builds 40 dictionary words (rank 21) — confidence **medium** (gloss disagreement across entries (top gloss share 59%) — possible homonymy/polysemy; needs linguist review; 20 occurrence(s) inside Parks compound stems glossed by inheritance from the form's own entries). Examples: *awarik ⟨uh-wuh-dih(k/t)⟩* 'throw, sling, toss.' (p.21); *icakawarik ⟨ih-chuh-kuh-wuh-dih(k/t)⟩* 'confuse, distract, as when taking someon' (p.54); *iriwiraawarik ⟨ih-dih-wih-dah-wuh-dih(k/t)⟩* 'roll out, as dough.' (p.68).
- **wuh** — 'put' — verb root (bound) — builds 12 dictionary words (rank 96) — confidence **medium** (gloss disagreement across entries (top gloss share 54%) — possible homonymy/polysemy; needs linguist review). Examples: *awuh ⟨uh-woo(h)⟩* 'stretch out tightly, as to stake a hide;' (p.26); *hawuh ⟨huh-woo(h)⟩* 'dye; dye black; ref. to dying a hide bla' (p.40); *icaawuh ⟨ih-chah-woo(h)⟩* 'hang on the end, put on the end,' (p.53).
- **cak** — 'strike' — VT (4) — builds 13 dictionary words (rank 75) — confidence **high** (5 occurrence(s) inside Parks compound stems glossed by inheritance from the form's own entries). Examples: *huuracak ⟨hoo-duh-chuh(k/t)⟩* 'puncture, punch a hole in.' (p.51); *kacak ⟨kuh-chuh(k/t)⟩* 'prick, as with a pointed object ; stab.' (p.75); *pakcak ⟨puhk-chuh(k/t)⟩* 'shoot, hit, strike, as with a bullet or ' (p.108).
- **aar** — 'become' — VP (1-i) — builds 56 dictionary words (rank 11) — confidence **high** (4 occurrence(s) inside Parks compound stems glossed by inheritance from the form's own entries). Examples: *akiihaaraar ⟨uh-kee-hah-dah(d)⟩* 'become lazy, indolent.' (p.11); *awiʔaar ⟨uh-wih-'ah(d)⟩* 'turn into, transform into.' (p.21); *kaahuraarikiriwitaar ⟨kah-hoo-dah-dih-kih-dih-wih-tah(d)⟩* 'weather to change.' (p.72).
- **kuut** — 'die' — VP (2) — builds 10 dictionary words (rank 114) — confidence **medium** (gloss disagreement across entries (top gloss share 43%) — possible homonymy/polysemy; needs linguist review; 2 occurrence(s) inside Parks compound stems glossed by inheritance from the form's own entries). Examples: *kickuut ⟨kihts-koot⟩* 'sour, spoil, rot, as a liquid, fruit, ve' (p.87); *riwahtiikuut ⟨dih-wuhh-tee-koot⟩* 'starve, starve to death, die of starvati' (p.147); *kuuctakaahak ⟨koots-tuh-kah-huh(k/t)⟩* 'fall unconscious.' (p.452).
- **iirik** — 'iirik' — noun root (bound) — builds 5 dictionary words (rank 232) — confidence **medium** (fewer than 3 direct Parks glosses and only 20% of host segmentations are high-confidence; 2 occurrence(s) inside Parks compound stems glossed by inheritance from the form's own entries). Examples: *raaʔiirik ⟨dah-'ee-dih(k/t)⟩* 'see, look at, as an activity or custom.' (p.115); *kiciirit ⟨kih-CHEE-diht⟩* 'variety of white, spotted, or yellow fli' (p.85); *ukaʔaatawiiriku ⟨oo-kuh-'ah-tuh-wee-dih-koo / oo-kah-tuh-wee-dih-koo⟩* 'mirror.' (p.170).
- **kiikaa** — 'drink' — VT (4) — builds 2 dictionary words (rank 588) — confidence **medium** (fewer than 3 direct Parks glosses and only 0% of host segmentations are high-confidence). Examples: *kiiraritkikaa ⟨kee-duh-diht-kih-kah⟩* 'drink soup.' (p.91); *kikaarik ⟨kih-kah-dih(k/t)⟩* 'water, give water to, as an animal; give' (p.93).

**Construction walkthrough**

*raʔuk ⟨duh-'oo(k/t)⟩* 'make' has 3sg *tiruʔ ⟨≈tih-doo'⟩* (Parks GO p.46 cites exactly this: *tiruʔ* 'he made it' < ti- + raʔuk); it also serves as the causative suffix *-raʔuk* (Unit 7). *raah ⟨dah(h)⟩* 'have, possess' (3sg *tiraa ⟨≈tih-dah⟩*) is the 'have' of BB L4 (*tah•ra*, see Unit 5) and appears in *ikstaah ⟨ihks-tah(h)⟩* 'have, carry, hold in one's hand' with *s* + *r* → *st* (Rule 21). *warik ⟨wuh-dih(k/t)⟩* gives *awarik ⟨uh-wuh-dih(k/t)⟩* 'throw' and *kaawarik ⟨kah-wuh-dih(k/t)⟩* 'put inside'; *wuh* gives *awuh ⟨uh-woo(h)⟩*, *kitawuh ⟨kih-tuh-woo(h)⟩* 'count', *huukitawuh ⟨hoo-kih-tuh-woo(h)⟩*. *cak ⟨chuhk / ih-chuh(k/t)⟩* 'strike' gives *pakcak ⟨puhk-chuh(k/t)⟩* 'shoot' (final *s* of *paks* lost, Rule 9R). *aar* is two dictionary verbs: *aar ⟨ah(d)⟩* 'become' (3sg *tiʔaa ⟨≈tih-'ah⟩*) and, with the preverb *ut-*, *aar ⟨ah(d)⟩* 'do' (3sg *tuutaa ⟨≈too-tah⟩*; BB L6 p.56 *ti•ari'* vs *tut•ari'*). *kuut ⟨koot⟩* 'die' is a passive (patient-marked) verb: 1sg *tikukuut ⟨≈tih-koo-koot⟩*. *kikak ⟨kih-kuh(k/t)⟩* 'cry' (3sg *tikikat ⟨≈tih-kih-kuht⟩*, BB L6 *ti•kikat*) and *kiikaa ⟨kee-kah⟩* 'drink' (3sg *tikiikaʔ ⟨≈tih-kee-kuh'⟩*, App.1 'to drink it') are low in the root ranking because few other dictionary stems are built on them, not because they are rare in use.

**Practice and answer key**

**Practice A — build the word.** Combine the pieces (Parks's own analysis) into the attested word. Watch for the sound changes listed in the walkthrough.

1. *a* 'cause to go' + *warik* 'in a line'  → ______ ('throw, sling, toss')
2. *paks* 'head' + *cak* 'strikes'  → ______ ('shoot, hit, strike, as with a bullet or an arrow')
3. *kita* 'on top' + *wuh* 'put'  → ______ ('count')
4. *kic* 'liquid' + *kuut* 'to die/ spoil'  → ______ ('sour, spoil, rot, as a liquid, fruit, vegetable')
5. *iks* 'hand' + *raah* 'to have'  → ______ ('have, carry, hold in one's hand')

**Practice B — break down the word.** Divide each attested word into its morphemes and gloss each.

1. *huukitawuh ⟨hoo-kih-tuh-woo(h)⟩* 'put on, put on top, as on a raised or high surface'  → ______
2. *kaawarik ⟨kah-wuh-dih(k/t)⟩* 'put inside, as in a dwelling'  → ______
3. *ickaraʔuk ⟨ihts-kuh-duh-'oo(k/t)⟩* 'sharpen, sharpen to a point'  → ______
4. *kaasik ⟨kah-sih(k/t)⟩* 'break, crack, as a nut shell'  → ______
5. *riwarik ⟨dih-wuh-dih(k/t)⟩* 'lose, be unable to find'  → ______

**Answer key (A and B)**

## Lesson 3.4 — qualities, colours and the natural world

**Objectives**

- Learn the descriptive roots *pahaat* 'red', *kaatiit* 'black', *taakaar* 'white', *rahkataar* 'yellow', *tariiʔuus* 'blue', *rihuur* 'big', *kiiʔac* 'long', *kucuu* 'big (in nouns)'.
- Learn the nature roots *kic* 'liquid/water', *haar* 'place', *huraar* 'ground', *sakuur* 'sun/day', *raa* 'way', *kiraar* 'liquid', *haas* 'string', *hiir* 'grass' and *hiir (uur...)* 'be good'.

(p.72); *raahiir ⟨dah-hee(d)⟩* 'be nice, good, as an event, occasion, ac' (p.117).

**Construction walkthrough**

Colour roots are descriptive verbs: *pahaat ⟨puh-haht⟩* 3sg *tipahaaʔat ⟨≈tih-puh-hah-'uht⟩* (Parks GO p.49: the final-syllable vowel is repeated with ʔ in non-subordinate forms), *kaatiit ⟨kah-teet⟩* 3sg *tikaatiiʔit ⟨≈tih-kah-tee-'iht⟩*, *taakaar ⟨tah-kah(d)⟩* 3sg *titaakaa ⟨≈tih-tah-kah⟩* (final *r* lost, Rule 23), *tariiʔuus ⟨tuh-dee-'oos⟩* 3sg *titariiʔuus ⟨≈tih-tuh-dee-'oos⟩*. The same roots close compound nouns: *Kicpahat ⟨KIHTS-puh-huht⟩* 'Red River', *kickaatiit ⟨KIHTS-kah-teet⟩* 'Mississippi River', *kictaakaa ⟨KIHTS-tah-kah⟩* 'gasoline', *asaapahat ⟨uh-sah-PUH-huht⟩* 'bay horse', *asaarahkata ⟨uh-sah-DUHH-kuh-tuh⟩* 'buckskin horse'. *rihuur ⟨dih-hoo(d)⟩* 'be big' is the verbal 'big' (*haakarihuur ⟨hah-kuh-dih-hoo(d)⟩*, *katarihuur ⟨kuh-tuh-dih-hoo(d)⟩* 'be wide'), *kucuu* the nominal one (*kiiskucuuʔ ⟨KEES-koo-choo'⟩* 'big liar, prevaricator , as one who habitually lies or exaggerates '). *kic* 'liquid, water' (rank 3) gives *kiicuʔ ⟨KEE-choo'⟩* 'water'; *haar* 'place' closes *kitahaaruʔ ⟨KIH-tuh-hah-doo'⟩* 'hilltop' and *hukahaaruʔ ⟨hoo-kuh-hah-doo'⟩*; *huraar* 'ground' gives *huraaruʔ ⟨hoo-dah-doo'⟩* and *kaahuraaruʔ ⟨KAH-hoo-dah-doo'⟩* 'world' (*kaa* + *huraar* + *uʔ*). *hiir* is two roots: 'grass' (*hiiruʔ ⟨HEE-doo'⟩*) and, only with the preverb *uur-*, 'be good' (*hiir ⟨hee(d)⟩* 3sg *tuuhii ⟨≈too-hee⟩*; App.1 'to be good'); the extraction splits them by the presence of *uur-* and by gloss.

**Blue Book cross-reference**

BB L2 p.33 'What colour is it?': *ti pahaat*, *ti tareus*, *ti kirareus*, and p.35 *rakis + rahkata = rakis•tahkata* 'a yellow stick'. ⚠ BB *ti pahaat* vs Parks *tipahaaʔat ⟨≈tih-puh-hah-'uht⟩*: Parks's 3sg has the reduplicated *-aaʔat* (GO p.49), BB writes the bare stem. ⚠ BB *tareus* 'blue' vs Parks *tariiʔuus ⟨tuh-dee-'oos⟩*: BB *e* for Parks *ii* and no ʔ. The sound change BB states on p.35 is Parks Rule 21 (*r* → *t* after an obstruent), seen in *asaarahkata ⟨uh-sah-DUHH-kuh-tuh⟩* only partly (here the *r* follows a vowel and stays).

**Practice and answer key**

**Practice A — build the word.** Combine the pieces (Parks's own analysis) into the attested word. Watch for the sound changes listed in the walkthrough.

1. *pahaat* 'red' + *kaatiit* 'dark'  → ______ ('be deep red, maroon, wine, oxblood')
2. *kic* 'liquid' + *taakaar* 'white'  → ______ ('gasoline')
3. *asaa* 'horse' + *pahaat* 'red'  → ______ ('bay horse')
4. *asaa* 'horse' + *rahkataar* 'yellow'  → ______ ('buckskin horse')
5. *kic* + *kaatiit* 'black water'  → ______ ('Mississippi River')

**Practice B — break down the word.** Divide each attested word into its morphemes and gloss each.

1. *haakarihuur ⟨hah-kuh-dih-hoo(d)⟩* 'have a large mouth, as a person'  → ______
2. *katarihuur ⟨kuh-tuh-dih-hoo(d)⟩* 'be wide'  → ______
3. *kaahuraaruʔ ⟨KAH-hoo-dah-doo'⟩* 'world'  → ______
4. *kitahaaruʔ ⟨KIH-tuh-hah-doo'⟩* 'hilltop, top of a hill'  → ______
5. *hukahaaruʔ ⟨hoo-kuh-hah-doo'⟩* 'bottom land, bottoms of a stream valley, as the low, level area on each side of a stream course, either a creek or a river '  → ______

**Answer key (A and B)**

A1. *pahaatkaatiit ⟨puh-haht-kah-teet⟩* (Parks p.107, `SK-pahaatkaatiit-p107-2051`) — segmentation confidence high
A2. *kictaakaa ⟨KIHTS-tah-kah⟩* (Parks p.436, `SK-kictaakaa-p436-3947`) — rules at the seams: 23 — segmentation confidence high
A3. *asaapahat ⟨uh-sah-PUH-huht⟩* (Parks p.362, `SK-asaapahat-p362-3741`) — rules at the seams: tolerance: vowel length/ʔ/h — segmentation confidence medium
A4. *asaarahkata ⟨uh-sah-DUHH-kuh-tuh⟩* (Parks p.362, `SK-asaarahkata-p362-3745`) — rules at the seams: 23, tolerance: vowel length/ʔ/h — segmentation confidence medium
A5. *kickaatiit ⟨KIHTS-kah-teet⟩* (Parks p.86, `SK-kickaatiit-p86-1612`) — segmentation confidence medium
B1. *haaka* 'mouth' + *rihuur* 'to be large' (Parks p.34, `SK-haakarihuur-p34-0576`) — confidence high
B2. *kata* 'against a surface' + *rihuur* 'be large' (Parks p.81, `SK-katarihuur-p81-1502`) — confidence high
B3. *kaa* 'land inside' + *huraar* + *uʔ* 'NOM -uʔ' (Parks p.72, `SK-kaahuraaruq-p72-1283`) — confidence medium
B4. *kita* 'on top' + *haar* 'place' + *uʔ* 'NOM -uʔ' (Parks p.98, `SK-kitahaaruq-p98-1910`) — confidence high
B5. *huka* 'in a shallow circumscribed space' + *haar* 'place' + *uʔ* 'NOM -uʔ' (Parks p.44, `SK-hukahaaruq-p44-0734`) — confidence high

**Where this deviates from the Blue Book, and why.** BB L2 teaches colours as the second lesson with the frame *ti ...*. Here they are vocabulary; the *ti-* prefix itself is taught in Lesson 5.1.

# Unit 4 — Making nouns

One affix at a time, applied only to roots from Units 1–3. Parks's nominal affixes are few (GO p.30–31, Tables 4–5): the absolutive *-uʔ*, the diminutive *-kis*, the augmentative *-kusuʔ*, the 'covering' *-kuukuʔuʔ*, the feminine *c-*, two cases (instrumental/locative *-hiriʔ*, locative *-kat*, *-ru*) and the body-part plural *-raar-*.

**Blue Book alignment.** BB L1 p.31 introduces *-u'*; BB L2 p.35 and L3 p.41–42 give the spelling rules that operate when roots are joined; BB L4 p.47 discusses plurality.

## Lesson 4.1 — the noun suffix -uʔ

**Objectives**

- Form free nouns from known roots with *-uʔ* (NOM).
- Recognise ʔ-insertion when the root ends in a vowel.

**New morphemes**

- NOM *-uʔ* — nominal absolutive: makes a free noun from a stem (no added meaning) — source: Parks GO p.31 Table 5 (iksuʔ < iks- + -uʔ); 677 dictionary etymologies name it; BB L1 p.31

**Construction walkthrough**

Parks: 'independent nouns are usually derived with the nominal absolutive suffix *-uʔ*' (GO p.30); the Blue Book says the same (BB p.31: “called nouns, and nouns in Pawnee often end in the suffix -u'.”). Consonant-final roots simply add it: *iksuʔ ⟨IHK-soo'⟩*, *asuʔ ⟨UH-soo'⟩*, *kiisuʔ ⟨KEE-soo'⟩*, *kaasuʔ ⟨KAH-soo'⟩*, *huraaruʔ ⟨hoo-dah-doo'⟩*, *akaaruʔ ⟨UH-kah-doo'⟩*, *taakaaruʔ ⟨TAH-kah-doo'⟩* 'bed sheet', *pahaatuʔ ⟨PUH-hah-too'⟩* 'red paint'. Vowel-final roots take a glottal stop before *-uʔ*: *haakaʔuʔ ⟨HAH-kuh-'oo'⟩*, *piiraʔuʔ ⟨PEE-duh-'oo'⟩*, *awiʔuʔ ⟨UH-wih-'oo'⟩*, *raaʔuʔ ⟨DAH-'oo'⟩* 'way, custom, tradition'. Short roots may lengthen: *haatuʔ ⟨HAH-too'⟩* 'tongue' from *hat*.

**Attested example words**

| Word | Class | Meaning (Parks) | Parks analysis | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **iksuʔ ⟨IHK-soo'⟩** | N | hand | iks 'hand' + uʔ 'NOM -uʔ' | Parks p.64, `SK-iksuq-p64-1111`; BB *iksu'* (L5, p.49) |
| **asuʔ ⟨UH-soo'⟩** | N | foot | as 'foot' + uʔ 'NOM -uʔ' | Parks p.18, `SK-asuq-p18-0202`; BB *asu'* (L5, p.49) |
| **paksuʔ ⟨PUHK-soo'⟩** | N | head | paks 'head' + uʔ 'NOM -uʔ' | Parks p.110, `SK-paksuq-p110-2116`; BB *paksu '* (L4) |
| **kiisuʔ ⟨KEE-soo'⟩** | N | bone | kiis 'bone' + uʔ 'NOM -uʔ' | Parks p.93, `SK-kiisuq-p93-1757` |
| **kaasuʔ ⟨KAH-soo'⟩** | N | leg | kaas 'leg' + uʔ 'NOM -uʔ' | Parks p.74, `SK-kaasuq-p74-1322` |
| **karaaruʔ ⟨kuh-DAH-doo'⟩** | N | belly, abdomen | karaar 'belly' + uʔ 'NOM -uʔ' | Parks p.77, `SK-karaaruq-p77-1409` |
| **huraaruʔ ⟨hoo-dah-doo'⟩** | N | land, ground | huraar + uʔ 'NOM -uʔ' | Parks p.47, `SK-huraaruq-p47-0790` |

| **raaʔuʔ ⟨DAH-'oo'⟩** | N | way, custom, tradition | raa 'way' + uʔ 'NOM -uʔ' | Parks p.115, `SK-raaquq-p115-2248` |
| **hiiruʔ ⟨HEE-doo'⟩** | N | grass, dry grass, hay | hiir 'grass' + uʔ 'NOM -uʔ' | Parks p.390, `SK-hiiruq-p390-3845` |

**Practice and answer key**

B5. *asaa* 'horse' + *akaar* 'dwelling' + *uʔ* 'NOM -uʔ' (Parks p.362, `SK-asaakaaruq-p362-3732`) — confidence high

## Lesson 4.2 — small and large: -kis, -kusuʔ, -kuukuʔuʔ

**Objectives**

- Form diminutives with *-kis* and see its two surface shapes (*-kis*, *-ki*).
- Know the augmentative and 'covering' suffixes from Parks Table 5.

**New morphemes**

- DIM *-kis* — diminutive; also simply forms free nouns — source: Parks GO p.31 Table 5 (asaakiʔ 'dog', riicikiʔ 'knife'); 58 etymologies — word-final *s* is often dropped (Parks Rule 9R): *-ki*
- AUG *-kusuʔ* — augmentative 'large, big' — source: Parks GO p.31 Table 5 (wiitakusuʔ 'large man') — ⚠ no dictionary etymology labels AUG; the only analysed example is Parks's own (data gap)
- *-kuukuʔuʔ* 'covering' — covering of N — source: Parks GO p.31 Table 5 (pakskuukuʔuʔ 'hat'); dictionary *kuuku* 'covering' root, 22 words

**Construction walkthrough**

*asaaki ⟨uh-SAH-kih⟩* 'dog' = *asaa* + *kis* with the final *s* lost (Rule 9R; Parks writes *asaakiʔ* in GO p.31 and *asaaki ⟨uh-SAH-kih⟩* in the entry). *rakis ⟨DUH-kihs⟩* = *rak* + *kis* with *kk* → *k* (Rule 20, degemination); *tiraakis ⟨TIH-dah-kihs⟩* 'bow' likewise. *karitki ⟨KUH-diht-kih⟩* 'rock, stone, pebble' and *haski ⟨HUHS-kih⟩* 'string, twine, cord, line' show *-ki*. The 'covering' suffix: *pakskuukuʔuʔ ⟨puhks-koo-koo-'oo'⟩* 'hat' (head-covering) and *haakarahkuukuʔuʔ ⟨HAH-kuh-duhh-koo-koo-'oo'⟩* 'bridle' (mouth + PL + covering + NOM).

**Attested example words**

| Word | Class | Meaning (Parks) | Parks analysis | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **asaaki ⟨uh-SAH-kih⟩** | N | dog | asaa 'dog' + kis 'DIM -kis' | Parks p.362, `SK-asaaki-p362-3736` |
| **rakis ⟨DUH-kihs⟩** | N | wood | rak 'wood' + kis 'DIM -kis' | Parks p.135, `SK-rakis-p135-2644`; BB *rakis* (L1, p.29) |
| **karitki ⟨KUH-diht-kih⟩** | N | rock, stone, pebble |  | Parks p.79, `SK-karitki-p79-1443`; BB *karitki* (L1, p.29) |
| **tiraakis ⟨TIH-dah-kihs⟩** | N | bow | tiraak 'bow/gun' + kis 'DIM -kis' | Parks p.166, `SK-tiraakis-p166-3268` |
| **kuucki ⟨koots-kih⟩** | N | kettle, pot , as for cooking | kuc 'vessel' + kis 'DIM -kis' | Parks p.452, `SK-kuucki-p452-4007` |
| **haski ⟨HUHS-kih⟩** | N | string, twine, cord, line | has 'string' + kis 'DIM -kis' | Parks p.37, `SK-haski-p37-0650` |
| **cuuraki ⟨choo-duh-kih⟩** | N | girl | cuura 'female' + kis 'DIM -kis' | Parks p.32, `SK-cuuraki-p32-0536`; BB *tsuraki* (L6, p.56) |
| **kisaacki ⟨KIH-sahts-kih⟩** | N | meat | kisaac 'meat' + kis 'DIM -kis' | Parks p.98, `SK-kisaacki-p98-1883`; BB *kisatski* (L11, p.79) |
| **kaciiki ⟨KUH-chee-kih⟩** | N | fish (generic) | kacii 'fish' + kis 'DIM -kis' | Parks p.75, `SK-kaciiki-p75-1358` |
| **paakis ⟨PAH-kihs⟩** | N | young buffalo bull | paak 'paak' + kis 'DIM -kis' | Parks p.454, `SK-paakis-p454-4053` |

| **haakarahkuukuʔuʔ ⟨HAH-kuh-duhh-koo-koo-'oo'⟩** | N | bridle | haaka 'mouth' + raar 'PL/ITER raar-' + kuuku 'covering' + uʔ 'NOM -uʔ' | Parks p.34, `SK-haakarahkuukuquq-p34-0572` |

## Lesson 4.3 — noun + noun compounds and what happens at the seam

**Objectives**

- Build compound nouns from two known roots plus *-uʔ*.
- Apply the boundary rules that appear most often in the data: *r* → *t* after an obstruent (Rule 21), *h*-loss (16), final *r*-loss (23), *t*+*r* → *h* (13–15), same-vowel contraction (5), ʔ-insertion.

**New morphemes**

- Rule 21 (*r* → *t* / obstruent _) — Parks Ch. 3 Rule 21; BB L2 p.35 and L3 p.41 rule 1 — source: sound_changes.py; 139 dictionary headwords explained by it
- Rule 16 (*h*-loss), Rule 23 (final *r*-loss), Rules 13–15 (*tr* → *hr* → *h*), Rule 5 (same-vowel contraction), ʔ-epenthesis — boundary adjustments — source: Parks Ch. 3; counts in review log

**Construction walkthrough**

*ikskiicuʔ ⟨ihks-KEE-choo'⟩* 'finger' = hand + neck + NOM with no change. *kiriktaakuʔ ⟨KIH-dihk-tah-koo'⟩* 'ash tree (Fraxinus spp.), the wood of which was used for pipestems and bows' = *kirik* + *raak* + *uʔ*: after *k* the *r* of *raak* becomes *t* (Rule 21) — the rule the Blue Book states as BB p.35: “rakis + rahkata = rakis•tahkata”. *kaastakis ⟨KAHS-tuh-kihs⟩* = *kaas* + *rakis*, same rule after *s*. *asaakaaruʔ ⟨UH-sah-kah-doo'⟩* 'barn' = *asaa* + *akaar* + *uʔ*: *aa* + *a* contract to *aa* (Rule 5). *Kiisaakaʔuʔ ⟨kee-SAH-kuh-'oo'⟩* '(formerly) Scalp Dance' = *kiis* + *haaka* + *uʔ*: the *h* of *haaka* is lost after *s* (Rule 16) and ʔ separates *a* from *uʔ*. *kictaakaa ⟨KIHTS-tah-kah⟩* 'gasoline' = *kic* + *taakaar*: word-final *r* is dropped (Rule 23). *asaakiraaʔ ⟨uh-SAH-kih-dah'⟩* 'light-colored horse' = *asaa* + *kiraar*: final *r* lost and a glottal stop closes the word.

**Attested example words**

| Word | Class | Meaning (Parks) | Parks analysis | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **ikskiicuʔ ⟨ihks-KEE-choo'⟩** | N | finger | iks 'hand' + kiic 'neck' + uʔ 'NOM -uʔ' | Parks p.63, `SK-ikskiicuq-p63-1094` |
| **haskiicuʔ ⟨HUHS-kee-choo'⟩** | N | string, cord, twine, thong , as used for a belt or to hold or carry something  | has 'string' + kiic 'neck' + uʔ 'NOM -uʔ' | Parks p.37, `SK-haskiicuq-p37-0653` |

**Blue Book cross-reference**

BB L3 p.41–42 spelling rules: BB p.41: “1. r changes to t after a hard consonant”; BB p.41: “2. w changes to p after a hard consonant”; BB p.41: “3. k drops off before p”; BB p.42: “4. r changes to h before any consonant”; BB p.42: “5. i + lay combine to make e”. Parks equivalents: rule 1 = Rule 21; rule 2 (*w* → *p*) is not among Parks's 24 numbered rules but is attested in 115 dictionary headwords (flagged as unnumbered); rule 3 (*k* drops before *p*) has no Parks counterpart in the engine ⚠; rule 4 corresponds to Rules 13–15 only before *r*/*h* (Parks has *r* → *h* in prefixes, Rule 12R) ⚠; rule 5 (*i* + *a* → *e*) corresponds to Parks Rule 7 (*i* + *a* → *ii*) — the two sources write the result differently ⚠.

**Practice and answer key**

## Lesson 4.4 — case and number on nouns: -hiriʔ, -kat, -ru, -raar-

**Objectives**

- Recognise the instrumental/locative *-hiriʔ*, the locatives *-kat* and *-ru*, and the plural *-raar-*.

**New morphemes**

- LOC/INST *-hiriʔ* (Parks Table 4 writes *-biriʔ*, an OCR shape for *-hiriʔ/-piriʔ*) — 'with, using; in, on, at' — source: Parks GO p.30 Table 4 (iksiriʔ 'by hand; on the hand'); 36 etymologies
- LOC *-kat* — 'in, on; among (animates)' — source: Parks GO p.30 (akahkat 'on the dwelling', asaakat 'among the horses')
- LOC *-ru* / *-wiru* — 'among, in the country of' (tribal and place names) — source: Parks GO p.30 (sahiiru 'in Cheyenne country')
- PL *-raar-* — plural of body-part and other nouns, before the case suffix — source: Parks GO p.30 (ikstaaririʔ 'with the hands'), p.43 (kiis- + raar- + ... 'bones'); 33 etymologies labelled PL — same shape as the iterative *raar-* (ITER) and the 3rd-person plural prefixes — Parks's gloss decides (ambiguous cases flagged)

**Construction walkthrough**

*kiihaahiriʔ ⟨kee-HAH-hih-dih'⟩* 'on the floor' = *kiihaar* 'room' + *hiriʔ* with *r* + *h* → *h* (Rules 14–15); *kitahaahiriʔ ⟨KIH-tuh-hah-hih-dih'⟩* 'on top' = *kita* + *haar* + *hiriʔ*; *ruksiriʔ ⟨dook-sih-dih'⟩* 'ten' = *ruks* + *hiriʔ* (the *h* is lost after *s*, Rule 16); *askusiriʔ ⟨uhs-koo-sih-dih'⟩* 'once'. *picikat ⟨PIH-chih-kuht⟩* 'in cold, frigid, freezing weather' (BB L15 *pitsikat*). *Kirikuurukstu ⟨kih-dih-koo-dooks-too⟩* 'Anadarko, Okla' carries *-ru*. The plural: *ckaraaruʔ ⟨TSKUH-dah-doo'⟩* 'clouds' = *icka* + *raar* + *uʔ*; *raktariikaʔuʔ ⟨DUHK-tuh-dee-kuh-'oo'⟩* 'gate, wooden gate' = *rak* + *raar* + *riika* + *uʔ* with *r* → *t* after *k* (Rule 21); *caahikstahkucuuʔ ⟨CHAH-hihks-tuhh-koo-choo'⟩* 'crowd' = people + PL + big.

**Attested example words**

| **kitahaahiriʔ ⟨KIH-tuh-hah-hih-dih'⟩** | ADV | on top, as on a dwelling or building | kita 'on top' + haar 'place + LOC' + hiriʔ 'LOC/INST -hiriʔ' | Parks p.98, `SK-kitahaahiriq-p98-1909` |

| **askusiriʔ ⟨uhs-koo-sih-dih'⟩** | ADV | once, one time | askus 'one' + hiriʔ 'LOC -hiriʔ' | Parks p.16, `SK-askusiriq-p16-0180` |
| **picikat ⟨PIH-chih-kuht⟩** | ADV | in cold, frigid, freezing weather | pici 'cold weather' + kat 'LOC -kat' | Parks p.111, `SK-picikat-p111-2162`; BB *pitsikat* (L15, p.95) |

| **ckaraaruʔ ⟨TSKUH-dah-doo'⟩** | N | clouds | icka 'cloud' + raar 'PL -raar' + uʔ 'NOM -uʔ' | Parks p.30, `SK-ckaraaruq-p30-0467` |

**Blue Book cross-reference**

BB p.47: “Plurality is usually indicated by markers in the introducer of” — the Blue Book treats plural as a verb-phrase matter (Unit 6), which agrees with Parks: nouns other than body parts have no plural marking (GO p.30).

# Unit 5 — The verb, part 1: mode, person, incorporation

Parks: every verb has a modal prefix and a stem; the person prefixes sit between them (GO p.33–35, Tables 7–9). The Blue Book calls the prefix block the 'introducer' (L4 p.45–46). This unit adds one prefix at a time to stems from Unit 3, using only the forms Parks cites.

**Blue Book alignment.** BB L4 (verb phrase), L6 (person markers, preverbs), L5 (passive introducers, physical possession) and L8 (nouns in a verb phrase).

## Lesson 5.1 — ti- : the third-person indicative frame

**Objectives**

- Read the dictionary's 3sg indicative (form 2) of known verbs as *ti-* + Ø + stem.
- Recognise the adjustments at the seam.

**New morphemes**

- IND *ti-* (3rd person agent, which is unmarked Ø) — indicative mode with a third-person subject: 'he/she/it ...' — source: Parks GO p.34 Table 8; every dictionary form 2 (2186 attested words); BB L4 p.46, L8 p.67 ('Pawnee does not use a letter or root to refer to the third person')

**Construction walkthrough**

BB p.46: “introducer•verb = mode + person + verb root” (BB). With a consonant-initial stem nothing changes: *tikikat ⟨≈tih-kih-kuht⟩* (*ti* + *kikak*; stem-final *k* → *t*, Parks GO p.46), *tikiikaʔ ⟨≈tih-kee-kuh'⟩*, *tiwiitit ⟨≈tih-wee-tiht⟩*, *tiwariiʔ ⟨≈tih-wuh-dee'⟩*, *tiraa ⟨≈tih-dah⟩*, *tikaaʔat ⟨≈tih-kah-'uht⟩*. With a vowel-initial stem a glottal stop is inserted: *tiʔat ⟨≈tih-'uht⟩* 'he went'. With *a*-initial stems *i* + *a* contract to *ii* (Rule 7): *tiirit ⟨≈tee-diht⟩* 'he is standing' (BB *t'erit*). Short *s*- and *k*-initial position stems lengthen the prefix vowel in Parks's forms: *tiiku ⟨≈tee-koo⟩* 'he is sitting' (final *s* dropped, Rule 9R), *tiisa ⟨≈tee-suh⟩* 'he is lying'. The 'make' stem *raʔuk* contracts: *tiruʔ ⟨≈tih-doo'⟩* (GO p.46).

**Dictionary paradigm forms** (Parks Part II of each entry)

| Stem (entry) | 1sg IND (form 1) | 3sg IND (form 2) | IMPF (form 3) | GER (form 4) | INT (form 5) | Parks p. |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **at ⟨uht⟩** 'go' | taatat ⟨≈tah-tuht⟩ | tiʔat ⟨≈tih-'uht⟩ | tiwuuʔ ⟨≈tih-woo'⟩ | iriiraata ⟨≈ih-dee-dah-tuh⟩ | tihuhta ⟨≈tih-hooh-tuh⟩ | 18 |
| **kus ⟨koo(s)⟩** 'be sitting, be seated' | tatku ⟨≈tuht-koo⟩ | tiiku ⟨≈tee-koo⟩ | — | iriiraaku ⟨≈ih-dee-dah-koo⟩ | tiikusta ⟨≈tee-koos-tuh⟩ | 102 |
| **sa ⟨suh⟩** 'be lying, reclined' | tacta ⟨≈tuhts-tuh⟩ | tiisa ⟨≈tee-suh⟩ | tiisaahuʔ ⟨≈tee-sah-hoo'⟩ | iriiraasa ⟨≈ih-dee-dah-suh⟩ | tisahista ⟨≈tih-suh-hihs-tuh⟩ | 150 |
| **arik ⟨uh-dih(k/t)⟩** 'be standing' | taatarit ⟨≈tah-tuh-diht⟩ | tiirit ⟨≈tee-diht⟩ | tiirikiʔ ⟨≈tee-dih-kih'⟩ | iriiraariki ⟨≈ih-dee-dah-dih-kih⟩ | tiirikista ⟨≈tee-dih-kihs-tuh⟩ | 13 |
| **wiitik ⟨wee-tih(k/t)⟩** 'sit down, be seated' | tatpiitit ⟨≈tuht-pee-tiht⟩ | tiwiitit ⟨≈tih-wee-tiht⟩ | tiwiitikuʔ ⟨≈tih-wee-tih-koo'⟩ | iriirawiitit ⟨≈ih-dee-duh-wee-tiht⟩ | tiwiitiksta ⟨≈tih-wee-tihks-tuh⟩ | 179 |
| **warii ⟨wuh-dee⟩** 'go around, walk around' | tatpariiʔ ⟨≈tuht-puh-dee'⟩ | tiwariiʔ ⟨≈tih-wuh-dee'⟩ | tiwariihuʔ ⟨≈tih-wuh-dee-hoo'⟩ | iriirawarii ⟨≈ih-dee-duh-wuh-dee⟩ | tiwariihusta ⟨≈tih-wuh-dee-hoos-tuh⟩ | 177 |
| **kikak ⟨kih-kuh(k/t)⟩** 'cry' | tatkikat ⟨≈tuht-kih-kuht⟩ | tikikat ⟨≈tih-kih-kuht⟩ | tikikaakuʔ ⟨≈tih-kih-kah-koo'⟩ | iriirakikat ⟨≈ih-dee-duh-kih-kuht⟩ | tikikaksta ⟨≈tih-kih-kuhks-tuh⟩ | 93 |
| **raʔuk ⟨duh-'oo(k/t)⟩** 'make' | tahuʔ ⟨≈tuh-hoo'⟩ | tiruʔ ⟨≈tih-doo'⟩ | tiruukuʔ ⟨≈tih-doo-koo'⟩ | iriiraraʔu ⟨≈ih-dee-duh-duh-'oo⟩ | tiruuksta ⟨≈tih-dooks-tuh⟩ | 114 |
| **kiikaa ⟨kee-kah⟩** 'drink' | tatkiikaʔ ⟨≈tuht-kee-kuh'⟩ | tikiikaʔ ⟨≈tih-kee-kuh'⟩ | tikiikaahuʔ ⟨≈tih-kee-kah-hoo'⟩ | iriirakikaa ⟨≈ih-dee-duh-kih-kah⟩ | tikiikaahista ⟨≈tih-kee-kah-hihs-tuh⟩ | 90 |
| **raah ⟨dah(h)⟩** 'have, possess' | tahaa ⟨≈tuh-hah⟩ | tiraa ⟨≈tih-dah⟩ | tiraahuʔ ⟨≈tih-dah-hoo'⟩ | iriiraraaha ⟨≈ih-dee-duh-dah-huh⟩ | tiraahista ⟨≈tih-dah-hihs-tuh⟩ | 116 |
| **kaaʔat ⟨kah-'uht⟩** 'go, walk inside, as in a dwelling or room' | tatkaaʔat ⟨≈tuht-kah-'uht⟩ | tikaaʔat ⟨≈tih-kah-'uht⟩ | tikaawuuʔ ⟨≈tih-kah-woo'⟩ | iriirakaata ⟨≈ih-dee-duh-kah-tuh⟩ | tikaahuhta ⟨≈tih-kah-hooh-tuh⟩ | 2 |
| **kaakus ⟨kah-koo(s)⟩** 'be inside , as a dwelling ' | tatkaaku ⟨≈tuht-kah-koo⟩ | tikaaku ⟨≈tih-kah-koo⟩ | tikaakuusuʔ ⟨≈tih-kah-koo-soo'⟩ | iriirakaaku ⟨≈ih-dee-duh-kah-koo⟩ | tikaakusta ⟨≈tih-kah-koos-tuh⟩ | 72 |

**Blue Book cross-reference**

BB p.67: “The u in +uks is a strong vowel which causes the a in modal” … (BB L8 p.67, on *ti* with the past marker). BB L6 p.55 *ti•kikat* = Parks *tikikat ⟨≈tih-kih-kuht⟩*; BB *ti•ruku'* 'he is making it' = Parks imperfective *tiruukuʔ ⟨≈tih-doo-koo'⟩* (Lesson 6.1).

**Practice and answer key**

**Practice C — derive the attested form.**

Given the dictionary stem, give the attested 3sg indicative (form 2).

C1. *kaaʔat ⟨kah-'uht⟩* ('go, walk inside, as in a dwelling or room') → ______
C2. *kaakus ⟨kah-koo(s)⟩* ('be inside , as a dwelling ') → ______
C3. *kaasa ⟨kah-suh⟩* ('be lying inside, as in a room ') → ______
C4. *kaarik ⟨kah-dih(k/t)⟩* ('be standing there') → ______
C5. *kitakus ⟨kih-tuh-koo(s)⟩* ('be sitting on top') → ______
C6. *hukaarik ⟨hoo-kah-dih(k/t)⟩* ('be standing in, as in a vehicle or conveyance, i.e., in a wagon box, wagon bed, truck bed, automobile') → ______

Answers (Parks Part II forms):

C1. *tikaaʔat ⟨≈tih-kah-'uht⟩* (Parks p.2, `SK-kaaqat-p2-0010`)
C2. *tikaaku ⟨≈tih-kah-koo⟩* (Parks p.72, `SK-kaakus-p72-1294`)
C3. *tikaasa ⟨≈tih-kah-suh⟩* (Parks p.73, `SK-kaasa-p73-1312`)
C4. *tikaarit ⟨≈tih-kah-diht⟩* (Parks p.73, `SK-kaarik-p73-1306`)
C5. *tikitaku ⟨≈tih-kih-tuh-koo⟩* (Parks p.98, `SK-kitakus-p98-1912`)
C6. *tihukaarit ⟨≈tih-hoo-kah-diht⟩* (Parks p.44, `SK-hukaarik-p44-0728`)

## Lesson 5.2 — ta- + t- 'I', ta- + s- 'you'

**Objectives**

- Form 1sg and 2sg indicatives from known stems: *ta-* + *t-*/*s-* + stem.
- Learn the junction changes: *t*+*r* → *h*, *t*+*w* → *tp*, *t*+*s* → *c*, *s*+*r* → *st*, *ta*+*t*+*a* → *taat*.

**New morphemes**

- IND *ta-* (1st/2nd person) — indicative with a speech-act-participant subject — source: Parks GO p.34 Table 8; 1602 dictionary form-1 words; BB L4 p.46 'ta ... I'm telling you'
- 1.A *t-* 'I' — first-person agent — source: Parks GO p.35 Table 9; BB L6 p.58 '+ t first person'
- 2.A *s-* 'you' — second-person agent — source: Parks GO p.35 Table 9; App.1 2sg cells (69 forms); BB L6 p.58 '+ s second person'

**Construction walkthrough**

Parks's own example (GO p.38): *taatat ⟨≈tah-tuht⟩* 'I went' < *ta-* + *t-* + *at*: *a* + *a* become long *aa* (Rule 5). The dictionary form 1 of each entry is this 1sg: *tatkikat ⟨≈tuht-kih-kuht⟩* (*tat* + *kikak*), *tatkiikaʔ ⟨≈tuht-kee-kuh'⟩*, *tatkaaʔat ⟨≈tuht-kah-'uht⟩*, *tatku ⟨≈tuht-koo⟩*. At the seam: *t* + *s* → *c* (*tacta ⟨≈tuhts-tuh⟩* 'I am lying', Rule 17, *s* → *c* after a consonant); *t* + *w* → *tp* (*tatpiitit ⟨≈tuht-pee-tiht⟩*, *tatpariiʔ ⟨≈tuht-puh-dee'⟩*; the *w* → *p* change BB states on p.41); *t* + *r* → *h* (*tahaa ⟨≈tuh-hah⟩* 'I have it', *tahuʔ ⟨≈tuh-hoo'⟩* 'I made it'; Rules 13–15). Second person replaces *t-* with *s-*: App.1 *taasat ⟨≈tah-suht⟩* 'you went', *taskiikaʔ ⟨≈tuhs-kee-kuh'⟩*, *tastaa ⟨≈tuhs-tah⟩* (*s* + *r* → *st*, Rule 21), *tasuuhii ⟨≈tuh-soo-hee⟩*.

**Dictionary paradigm forms** (Parks Part II of each entry)

| Stem (entry) | 1sg IND (form 1) | 3sg IND (form 2) | IMPF (form 3) | GER (form 4) | INT (form 5) | Parks p. |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **kikak ⟨kih-kuh(k/t)⟩** 'cry' | tatkikat ⟨≈tuht-kih-kuht⟩ | tikikat ⟨≈tih-kih-kuht⟩ | tikikaakuʔ ⟨≈tih-kih-kah-koo'⟩ | iriirakikat ⟨≈ih-dee-duh-kih-kuht⟩ | tikikaksta ⟨≈tih-kih-kuhks-tuh⟩ | 93 |
| **kiikaa ⟨kee-kah⟩** 'drink' | tatkiikaʔ ⟨≈tuht-kee-kuh'⟩ | tikiikaʔ ⟨≈tih-kee-kuh'⟩ | tikiikaahuʔ ⟨≈tih-kee-kah-hoo'⟩ | iriirakikaa ⟨≈ih-dee-duh-kih-kah⟩ | tikiikaahista ⟨≈tih-kee-kah-hihs-tuh⟩ | 90 |
| **kaaʔat ⟨kah-'uht⟩** 'go, walk inside, as in a dwelling or room' | tatkaaʔat ⟨≈tuht-kah-'uht⟩ | tikaaʔat ⟨≈tih-kah-'uht⟩ | tikaawuuʔ ⟨≈tih-kah-woo'⟩ | iriirakaata ⟨≈ih-dee-duh-kah-tuh⟩ | tikaahuhta ⟨≈tih-kah-hooh-tuh⟩ | 2 |
| **kus ⟨koo(s)⟩** 'be sitting, be seated' | tatku ⟨≈tuht-koo⟩ | tiiku ⟨≈tee-koo⟩ | — | iriiraaku ⟨≈ih-dee-dah-koo⟩ | tiikusta ⟨≈tee-koos-tuh⟩ | 102 |
| **sa ⟨suh⟩** 'be lying, reclined' | tacta ⟨≈tuhts-tuh⟩ | tiisa ⟨≈tee-suh⟩ | tiisaahuʔ ⟨≈tee-sah-hoo'⟩ | iriiraasa ⟨≈ih-dee-dah-suh⟩ | tisahista ⟨≈tih-suh-hihs-tuh⟩ | 150 |
| **wiitik ⟨wee-tih(k/t)⟩** 'sit down, be seated' | tatpiitit ⟨≈tuht-pee-tiht⟩ | tiwiitit ⟨≈tih-wee-tiht⟩ | tiwiitikuʔ ⟨≈tih-wee-tih-koo'⟩ | iriirawiitit ⟨≈ih-dee-duh-wee-tiht⟩ | tiwiitiksta ⟨≈tih-wee-tihks-tuh⟩ | 179 |
| **warii ⟨wuh-dee⟩** 'go around, walk around' | tatpariiʔ ⟨≈tuht-puh-dee'⟩ | tiwariiʔ ⟨≈tih-wuh-dee'⟩ | tiwariihuʔ ⟨≈tih-wuh-dee-hoo'⟩ | iriirawarii ⟨≈ih-dee-duh-wuh-dee⟩ | tiwariihusta ⟨≈tih-wuh-dee-hoos-tuh⟩ | 177 |
| **raah ⟨dah(h)⟩** 'have, possess' | tahaa ⟨≈tuh-hah⟩ | tiraa ⟨≈tih-dah⟩ | tiraahuʔ ⟨≈tih-dah-hoo'⟩ | iriiraraaha ⟨≈ih-dee-duh-dah-huh⟩ | tiraahista ⟨≈tih-dah-hihs-tuh⟩ | 116 |
