**Blue Book cross-reference**

BB L4 p.46: *tah•ra* 'I have it', *tas•ta* 'you have it', *ti ra* 'he has it'. Parks App.1 'to have it': *tahaa ⟨≈tuh-hah⟩*, *tastaa ⟨≈tuhs-tah⟩*, *tiraa ⟨≈tih-dah⟩*. ⚠ BB keeps *r* after *h* (*tahra*) where Parks writes *tahaa* (the *r* is absorbed, Rule 15); BB *tas•ta* agrees with Parks *tastaa ⟨≈tuhs-tah⟩* apart from vowel length. BB L6 p.58 *tat•kikat* 'I am crying' = Parks *tatkikat ⟨≈tuht-kih-kuht⟩*.

**Practice and answer key**

**Practice C — derive the attested form.**

Given the 3sg indicative of each verb, give the attested 1sg indicative.

C1. *tikikat ⟨≈tih-kih-kuht⟩* ('cry', stem *kikak ⟨kih-kuh(k/t)⟩*) → ______
C2. *tiwariiʔ ⟨≈tih-wuh-dee'⟩* ('go around, walk around', stem *warii ⟨wuh-dee⟩*) → ______
C3. *tiiku ⟨≈tee-koo⟩* ('be sitting, be seated', stem *kus ⟨koo(s)⟩*) → ______
C4. *tiruʔ ⟨≈tih-doo'⟩* ('make', stem *raʔuk ⟨duh-'oo(k/t)⟩*) → ______
C5. *tikaaʔat ⟨≈tih-kah-'uht⟩* ('go, walk inside, as in a dwelling or room', stem *kaaʔat ⟨kah-'uht⟩*) → ______

Answers (Parks Part II forms):

C1. *tatkikat ⟨≈tuht-kih-kuht⟩* (Parks p.93, `SK-kikak-p93-1767`)
C2. *tatpariiʔ ⟨≈tuht-puh-dee'⟩* (Parks p.177, `SK-warii-p177-3506`)
C3. *tatku ⟨≈tuht-koo⟩* (Parks p.102, `SK-kus-p102-1993`)
C4. *tahuʔ ⟨≈tuh-hoo'⟩* (Parks p.114, `SK-raquk-p114-2221`)
C5. *tatkaaʔat ⟨≈tuht-kah-'uht⟩* (Parks p.2, `SK-kaaqat-p2-0010`)

## Lesson 5.3 — ku- 'me', a- 'you' — the patient prefixes

**Objectives**

- Recognise verbs whose subject is grammatically a patient (Parks's 'passive' class, VP).
- Read *ti-* + *ku-* 'it ... me' and *ta-* + *a-* 'it ... you'.

**New morphemes**

- 1.P *ku-* 'me, us' — first-person patient — source: Parks GO p.35 Table 9; dictionary form 1 of VP verbs (e.g. tikuʔaa); BB L5 p.51 'passive introducers', L6 p.58 '+ ku'
- 2.P *a-* 'you' — second-person patient — source: Parks GO p.35 Table 9; App.1 'to be sick' 2sg; BB L6 p.58 '+ a second person to you'

**Construction walkthrough**

Parks: passive verbs 'describe actions that befall people ... what is interpretively a subject is grammatically a patient' (GO p.32). So 'I became' is *tikuʔaa ⟨≈tih-koo-'ah⟩* = *ti* + *ku* 'me' + *aar*, and 'I died' is *tikukuut ⟨≈tih-koo-koot⟩*; 'I forgot' *tikuuciksurahac ⟨≈tih-koo-chihk-soo-duh-huhts⟩*, 'I am thirsty' *tikuucikstahiis ⟨≈tih-koo-chihks-tuh-hees⟩* (*ku* + *aciks*: the *u* dominates the following vowel — Rule 6; BB L5 p.50: When i follows u, it drops out. So ti + ku + iks = tiku 'ks,). App.1 'to be sick': *tikukiraawaʔ ⟨≈tih-koo-kih-dah-wuh'⟩* 'I am sick', *takiraawaʔ ⟨≈tuh-kih-dah-wuh'⟩* 'you are sick' (*ta* + *a*), *tihkiraawaʔ ⟨≈tihh-kih-dah-wuh'⟩*.

**Dictionary paradigm forms** (Parks Part II of each entry)

| Stem (entry) | 1sg IND (form 1) | 3sg IND (form 2) | IMPF (form 3) | GER (form 4) | INT (form 5) | Parks p. |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **aar ⟨ah(d)⟩** 'become' | tikuʔaa ⟨≈tih-koo-'ah⟩ | tiʔaa ⟨≈tih-'ah⟩ | tiʔaariʔ ⟨≈tih-'ah-dih'⟩ | iriiraʔaara ⟨≈ih-dee-duh-'ah-duh⟩ | tiʔaarista ⟨≈tih-'ah-dihs-tuh⟩ | 351 |
| **kuut ⟨koot⟩** 'die' | tikukuut ⟨≈tih-koo-koot⟩ | tikuut ⟨≈tih-koot⟩ | tikuutuʔ ⟨≈tih-koo-too'⟩ | iriirakuuti ⟨≈ih-dee-duh-koo-tih⟩ | tikuutista ⟨≈tih-koo-tihs-tuh⟩ | 452 |
| **hurahac ⟨hoo-duh-huhts⟩** 'disappear' | tikuhurahac ⟨≈tih-koo-hoo-duh-huhts⟩ | tihurahac ⟨≈tih-hoo-duh-huhts⟩ | tihurahaacuʔ ⟨≈tih-hoo-duh-hah-choo'⟩ | iriirahurahac ⟨≈ih-dee-duh-hoo-duh-huhts⟩ | tihurahacista ⟨≈tih-hoo-duh-huh-chihs-tuh⟩ | 47 |
| **kaahikuut ⟨kah-hih-koot⟩** 'die of old age' | tikukaahikuut ⟨≈tih-koo-kah-hih-koot⟩ | tikaahikuut ⟨≈tih-kah-hih-koot⟩ | tikaahikuutuʔ ⟨≈tih-kah-hih-koo-too'⟩ | iriirakaahikuuti ⟨≈ih-dee-duh-kah-hih-koo-tih⟩ | tikaahikuutista ⟨≈tih-kah-hih-koo-tihs-tuh⟩ | 2 |
| **aciksurahac ⟨uh-chihk-soo-duh-huhts⟩** 'forget' | tikuuciksurahac ⟨≈tih-koo-chihk-soo-duh-huhts⟩ | tiiciksurahac ⟨≈tee-chihk-soo-duh-huhts⟩ | tiiciksurahaacuʔ ⟨≈tee-chihk-soo-duh-hah-choo'⟩ | iriiraaciksurahac ⟨≈ih-dee-dah-chihk-soo-duh-huhts⟩ | tiiciksurahacista ⟨≈tee-chihk-soo-duh-huh-chihs-tuh⟩ | 8 |
| **acikstaahiis ⟨uh-chihks-tah-hees⟩** 'throat to be dry' | tikuucikstahiis ⟨≈tih-koo-chihks-tuh-hees⟩ | tiicikstahiis ⟨≈tee-chihks-tuh-hees⟩ | — | iriiraacikstaahiisu ⟨≈ih-dee-dah-chihks-tah-hee-soo⟩ | — | 7 |
| **kisuʔaar ⟨kih-soo-'ah(d)⟩** 'become slender, thin' | tikukisuʔaa ⟨≈tih-koo-kih-soo-'ah⟩ | tikisuʔaa ⟨≈tih-kih-soo-'ah⟩ | tikisuʔaariʔ ⟨≈tih-kih-soo-'ah-dih'⟩ | iriirakisuʔaara ⟨≈ih-dee-duh-kih-soo-'ah-duh⟩ | tikisuʔaarista ⟨≈tih-kih-soo-'ah-dihs-tuh⟩ | 98 |
| **pahitaar ⟨puh-hih-tah(d)⟩** 'become quiet, still' | tikupahitaa ⟨≈tih-koo-puh-hih-tah⟩ | tipahitaa ⟨≈tih-puh-hih-tah⟩ | tipahitaariʔ ⟨≈tih-puh-hih-tah-dih'⟩ | iriirapahitaara ⟨≈ih-dee-duh-puh-hih-tah-duh⟩ | tipahitaarista ⟨≈tih-puh-hih-tah-dihs-tuh⟩ | 108 |

## Lesson 5.4 — putting a body part inside the verb; ri- physical possession

**Objectives**

- Combine the body roots of Unit 2 with the position and locative roots of Unit 3 inside one verb.
- Recognise the possession prefix *ri-* (and *uur-*) in the Blue Book's body dialogues.

**New morphemes**

- Incorporated noun (slot 25) — body parts, natural phenomena, foods and cultural products are placed before the stem and drop their *-uʔ* — source: Parks GO p.43; Table 7 slot 25; BB L8 p.67–68 'Nouns in a verb phrase'
- PHY.POSS *ri-* and P.POSS *uur-* — the subject physically possesses the noun — source: Parks GO p.36 (taruuraariit 'he has it (standing)'; tatiriiriikstahka); BB L5 p.51

**Construction walkthrough**

Parks GO p.43: 'An incorporated noun drops the nominal suffix of its independent form'. So *iks-* 'hand' + *kaa* 'be inside' = *ikskaa ⟨ihks-kah⟩* 'have one's hand in, as a glove or drawer' (3sg *tiikskaa ⟨≈teeks-kah⟩*, 1sg *taatikskaa ⟨≈tah-tihks-kah⟩*); *icka-* + *kaa* = *ickakaa ⟨ihts-kuh-kah⟩* 'be wearing a mask, have a mask on' (3sg *tiickakaa ⟨≈teets-kuh-kah⟩*); *iks* + *ta* 'be hanging' = *iksta ⟨ihks-tuh⟩* 'be a handle'; *kiis* + *ta* = *kiista ⟨kees-tuh⟩* 'be lame, crippled'; *haas* + *ta* = *haasta ⟨hahs-tuh⟩*; *iks* + *raah* = *ikstaah ⟨ihks-tah(h)⟩* (Rule 21), 3sg *tiʔikstaa ⟨≈tih-'ihks-tah⟩*. *as* + *wiitik* = *aspiitik ⟨uhs-pee-tih(k/t)⟩* 'set, put one's foot down' with *w* → *p* after *s*. Possession: Parks's *taruuraariit* 'he has it (something standing)' is *ti-* + *a-* + *ri-* + *uur-* + *ariik* (GO p.36); the Blue Book frame is BB p.51: “mode + phys. poss. + person + noun + verb”.

**Attested example words**

| **ickakaa ⟨ihts-kuh-kah⟩** | VL | be wearing a mask, have a mask on | icka 'face' + kaa 'to be inside' | Parks p.57, `SK-ickakaa-p57-0978` |

| **kiickaa ⟨keets-kah⟩** | VL | be around one's neck | kiic 'neck' + kaa 'to be inside' | Parks p.89, `SK-kiickaa-p89-1663` |
| **aspiitik ⟨uhs-pee-tih(k/t)⟩** | VI (4) | set, put one's foot down | as 'foot' + wiitik 'set down' | Parks p.17, `SK-aspiitik-p17-0186` |

Treat the BB sentences as BB-attested, not dictionary-attested.

## Lesson 5.5 — the preverbs ut- and uur-

**Objectives**

- Recognise discontinuous stems: a preverb among the prefixes plus a stem base (Parks GO p.31).
- Contrast pairs that differ only in the preverb.

**New morphemes**

- PREV *ut-* — part of the stem (no meaning of its own); also benefactive 'for, to' — source: Parks GO p.31, p.37; 345 dictionary entries; BB L6 p.56, L10 p.75
- PREV *uur-* — part of the stem; also patient possession — source: Parks GO p.31, p.36; 180 dictionary entries; BB L10 p.75 '+ur'
- PREV *ir-* / *a-* — part of the stem (e.g. 'come'); first/second person *ir-*, third person *a-* — source: Parks GO p.31–32 (a (ir-...) 'to come'); 200 dictionary entries

**Construction walkthrough**

Same base, different stem: *aar ⟨ah(d)⟩* 'become' has 3sg *tiʔaa ⟨≈tih-'ah⟩*; *aar ⟨ah(d)⟩* 'do' with *ut-* has 3sg *tuutaa ⟨≈too-tah⟩* (*ti* + *ut* → *tuut*: the *u* dominates, Rules 1R/6; BB L10 p.75 'i +u becomes u'). *raah ⟨dah(h)⟩* 'have' 3sg *tiraa ⟨≈tih-dah⟩*; *raah ⟨dah(h)⟩* 'owe, owe to' with *uur-* 3sg *turaa ⟨≈too-dah⟩*. *awuh ⟨uh-woo(h)⟩* 'stretch out tightly, as to stake a hide' 3sg *tiiwu ⟨≈tee-woo⟩* versus *awuh ⟨uh-woo(h)⟩* 'draw, paint a picture' 3sg *tuurawu ⟨≈too-duh-woo⟩*. *iirik ⟨ee-dih(k/t)⟩* 'see' is always with *ut-*: 3sg *tuutiirit ⟨≈too-tee-diht⟩*, 1sg *tatuutiirit ⟨≈tuh-too-tee-diht⟩* (BB L14 *Tatut•erit* 'I saw it'). *hiir ⟨hee(d)⟩* 'be good' is always with *uur-*: 3sg *tuuhii ⟨≈too-hee⟩*, 1sg *tatuuhii ⟨≈tuh-too-hee⟩* (App.1). Three homonymous 'kaakus' stems differ only by preverb: *tikaaku ⟨≈tih-kah-koo⟩* 'be inside , as a dwelling ', *tutkaaku ⟨≈toot-kah-koo⟩* 'have as an in-law (male speaking)', *tuuhkaaku ⟨≈tooh-kah-koo⟩* 'live, stay with'.

**Dictionary paradigm forms** (Parks Part II of each entry)

| Stem (entry) | 1sg IND (form 1) | 3sg IND (form 2) | IMPF (form 3) | GER (form 4) | INT (form 5) | Parks p. |

| **aar ⟨ah(d)⟩** 'do' | tatuutaa ⟨≈tuh-too-tah⟩ | tuutaa ⟨≈too-tah⟩ | tuutariʔ ⟨≈too-tuh-dih'⟩ | iriruutaara ⟨≈ih-dih-doo-tah-duh⟩ | tuutarista ⟨≈too-tuh-dihs-tuh⟩ | 351 |
| **raah ⟨dah(h)⟩** 'have, possess' | tahaa ⟨≈tuh-hah⟩ | tiraa ⟨≈tih-dah⟩ | tiraahuʔ ⟨≈tih-dah-hoo'⟩ | iriiraraaha ⟨≈ih-dee-duh-dah-huh⟩ | tiraahista ⟨≈tih-dah-hihs-tuh⟩ | 116 |

**Blue Book cross-reference**

BB p.56: “ti•ari' he's becoming” vs *tut•ari'* 'he's making it or doing it' (BB L6 p.56) = Parks *tiʔaa ⟨≈tih-'ah⟩* / *tuutaa ⟨≈too-tah⟩* (⚠ BB writes the imperfective *-i'*; Parks's perfective 3sg is *tuutaa ⟨≈too-tah⟩*, imperfective *tuutariʔ ⟨≈too-tuh-dih'⟩*). BB L10 p.75 *tuh•ra'* 'he is using it' = Parks *turaa ⟨≈too-dah⟩*.

**Practice and answer key**

**Practice C — derive the attested form.**

Given the 3sg indicative (with preverb) of each verb, give the attested 1sg indicative.

C1. *tuutaa ⟨≈too-tah⟩* ('do', stem *aar ⟨ah(d)⟩*) → ______
C2. *turaa ⟨≈too-dah⟩* ('owe, owe to', stem *raah ⟨dah(h)⟩*) → ______
C3. *tuutiirit ⟨≈too-tee-diht⟩* ('see, look at', stem *iirik ⟨ee-dih(k/t)⟩*) → ______

C5. *tutkaaku ⟨≈toot-kah-koo⟩* ('have as an in-law (male speaking)', stem *kaakus ⟨kah-koo(s)⟩*) → ______

Answers (Parks Part II forms):

C1. *tatuutaa ⟨≈tuh-too-tah⟩* (Parks p.351, `SK-aar-p351-3598`)
C2. *taturaa ⟨≈tuh-too-dah⟩* (Parks p.116, `SK-raah-p116-2265`)

C5. *tatutkaaku ⟨≈tuh-toot-kah-koo⟩* (Parks p.72, `SK-kaakus-p72-1295`)

| **katarihuur ⟨kuh-tuh-dih-hoo(d)⟩** | VD | be wide | kata 'against a surface' + rihuur 'be large' | Parks p.81, `SK-katarihuur-p81-1502` |

| **katiihaʔuk ⟨kuh-tee-huh-'oo(k/t)⟩** | VT (3) | blacken, make black, color black | katiit 'black' + raʔuk 'CAUS -raʔuk' | Parks p.82, `SK-katiihaquk-p82-1530` |
| **katiituʔ ⟨kuh-TEE-too'⟩** | N | black | katiit 'black' + uʔ 'NOM -uʔ' | Parks p.83, `SK-katiituq-p83-1537` |
| **kica ⟨kih-chuh⟩** | VI (4) | be water (in a location), either running or still, as a puddle, creek, etc | kic 'liquid' + a 'be' | Parks p.84, `SK-kica-p84-1569` |
| **kicaar ⟨kih-chah(d)⟩** | VI (1-i) | become watery | kic 'liquid' + aar 'become' | Parks p.84, `SK-kicaar-p84-1573` |
| **kickaa ⟨kihts-kah⟩** | VL | be liquid in a container, as water in a pail | kic 'water' + kaa 'to be in' | Parks p.86, `SK-kickaa-p86-1606` |
| **kickiraar ⟨kihts-kih-dah(d)⟩** | VD | be clear water | kic 'liquid' + kiraar 'to be clear' | Parks p.87, `SK-kickiraar-p87-1634` |

Answer key (Parks's analysis, sound rules fired, confidence):

- *takus ⟨tuh-koo(s)⟩* = ut 'PREV ut-' + ta 'sitting' + kus 'suspended' — no change at the seams — high (Parks p.158, `SK-takus-p158-3089`)
- *hiihtakus ⟨heeh-tuh-koo(s)⟩* = hiir 'grass' + ta 'GEN.LOC ta-' + kus 'to be sitting' — rules: 23 — high (Parks p.389, `SK-hiihtakus-p389-3822`)
- *aku ⟨uh-koo⟩* = a 'and' + ku 'perhaps' — no change at the seams — high (Parks p.11, `SK-aku-p11-0127`)
- *ickahak ⟨ihts-kuh-huh(k/t)⟩* = icka 'face' + hak 'to pass by' — no change at the seams — high (Parks p.57, `SK-ickahak-p57-0976`)
- *katarihuur ⟨kuh-tuh-dih-hoo(d)⟩* = kata 'against a surface' + rihuur 'be large' — no change at the seams — high (Parks p.81, `SK-katarihuur-p81-1502`)
- *katawiitik ⟨kuh-tuh-wee-tih(k/t)⟩* = kata 'against a vertical surface' + wiitik 'sit down' — no change at the seams — high (Parks p.82, `SK-katawiitik-p82-1527`)
- *katiihaʔuk ⟨kuh-tee-huh-'oo(k/t)⟩* = katiit 'black' + raʔuk 'CAUS -raʔuk' — rules: 13, 15 — high (Parks p.82, `SK-katiihaquk-p82-1530`)
- *katiituʔ ⟨kuh-TEE-too'⟩* = katiit 'black' + uʔ 'NOM -uʔ' — no change at the seams — high (Parks p.83, `SK-katiituq-p83-1537`)
- *kica ⟨kih-chuh⟩* = kic 'liquid' + a 'be' — no change at the seams — high (Parks p.84, `SK-kica-p84-1569`)
- *kicaar ⟨kih-chah(d)⟩* = kic 'liquid' + aar 'become' — no change at the seams — high (Parks p.84, `SK-kicaar-p84-1573`)
- *kickaa ⟨kihts-kah⟩* = kic 'water' + kaa 'to be in' — no change at the seams — high (Parks p.86, `SK-kickaa-p86-1606`)
- *kickiraar ⟨kihts-kih-dah(d)⟩* = kic 'liquid' + kiraar 'to be clear' — no change at the seams — high (Parks p.87, `SK-kickiraar-p87-1634`)

**Unit 5 cumulative paradigm drill.** Appendix 1 *to drink it*, indicative: 1sg *tatkiikaʔ ⟨≈tuht-kee-kuh'⟩*, 2sg *taskiikaʔ ⟨≈tuhs-kee-kuh'⟩*, 3sg *tikiikaʔ ⟨≈tih-kee-kuh'⟩* (App.1 p.6). Identify mode prefix, person prefix and stem in each.

# Unit 6 — The verb, part 2: aspect, number, time, negation

Suffixes mark aspect and subordination (Parks GO p.44–48, Tables 16–17); number is marked by the proclitic *si-* and the prefixes *rak-*, *raar-*, *ir-*, *ak-* (GO p.35–36); the aorist/jussive *uks-* marks past time and commands (GO p.38).

**Blue Book alignment.** BB L5 (completed/incomplete action, commands, irregular roots), L4 (plurals), L8 (past tense), L15 (verb-final k), L19 (future), L1/L4 (indicators ka/kaki).

## Lesson 6.1 — the imperfective -huʔ (-:hus)

**Objectives**

- Form 'is ...-ing' from known verbs (dictionary form 3).
- See why a stem-final *k* appears only before a suffix.

**New morphemes**

- IMPF *-huʔ* / *-:hus* — imperfective 'uncompleted, in progress'; lengthens the stem-final vowel; final *s* dropped word-finally — source: Parks GO p.46–47, Table 17; 1255 dictionary form-3 words; BB L5 p.52–53

**Construction walkthrough**

Parks GO p.47: *tikacaakuʔ* 'he is cutting it' vs *tikacat* 'he cut it'. In the dictionary: *tikikat ⟨≈tih-kih-kuht⟩* 'he cried' → *tikikaakuʔ ⟨≈tih-kih-kah-koo'⟩* 'he is crying' (the stem-final *k* of *kikak* surfaces before the suffix, and *huʔ* after *k* appears as *-uʔ* with the *h* lost, Rule 16); *tiruʔ ⟨≈tih-doo'⟩* → *tiruukuʔ ⟨≈tih-doo-koo'⟩*; *tikiikaʔ ⟨≈tih-kee-kuh'⟩* → *tikiikaahuʔ ⟨≈tih-kee-kah-hoo'⟩* (Parks GO p.47 cites this pair); *tiraa ⟨≈tih-dah⟩* → *tiraahuʔ ⟨≈tih-dah-hoo'⟩*; *tiwariiʔ ⟨≈tih-wuh-dee'⟩* → *tiwariihuʔ ⟨≈tih-wuh-dee-hoo'⟩*; *tiwiitit ⟨≈tih-wee-tiht⟩* → *tiwiitikuʔ ⟨≈tih-wee-tih-koo'⟩*; *tiisa ⟨≈tee-suh⟩* → *tiisaahuʔ ⟨≈tee-sah-hoo'⟩*. Verbs of class 1-i and 2-i take *-iʔ* instead: *tiirit ⟨≈tee-diht⟩* → *tiirikiʔ ⟨≈tee-dih-kih'⟩*, *tiʔaa ⟨≈tih-'ah⟩* → *tiʔaariʔ ⟨≈tih-'ah-dih'⟩*. 'Go' is suppletive in the imperfective: *tiʔat ⟨≈tih-'uht⟩* → *tiwuuʔ ⟨≈tih-woo'⟩*, *tikaaʔat ⟨≈tih-kah-'uht⟩* → *tikaawuuʔ ⟨≈tih-kah-woo'⟩*.

**Dictionary paradigm forms** (Parks Part II of each entry)

| Stem (entry) | 1sg IND (form 1) | 3sg IND (form 2) | IMPF (form 3) | GER (form 4) | INT (form 5) | Parks p. |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **kikak ⟨kih-kuh(k/t)⟩** 'cry' | tatkikat ⟨≈tuht-kih-kuht⟩ | tikikat ⟨≈tih-kih-kuht⟩ | tikikaakuʔ ⟨≈tih-kih-kah-koo'⟩ | iriirakikat ⟨≈ih-dee-duh-kih-kuht⟩ | tikikaksta ⟨≈tih-kih-kuhks-tuh⟩ | 93 |
| **raʔuk ⟨duh-'oo(k/t)⟩** 'make' | tahuʔ ⟨≈tuh-hoo'⟩ | tiruʔ ⟨≈tih-doo'⟩ | tiruukuʔ ⟨≈tih-doo-koo'⟩ | iriiraraʔu ⟨≈ih-dee-duh-duh-'oo⟩ | tiruuksta ⟨≈tih-dooks-tuh⟩ | 114 |
| **kiikaa ⟨kee-kah⟩** 'drink' | tatkiikaʔ ⟨≈tuht-kee-kuh'⟩ | tikiikaʔ ⟨≈tih-kee-kuh'⟩ | tikiikaahuʔ ⟨≈tih-kee-kah-hoo'⟩ | iriirakikaa ⟨≈ih-dee-duh-kih-kah⟩ | tikiikaahista ⟨≈tih-kee-kah-hihs-tuh⟩ | 90 |

| **aar ⟨ah(d)⟩** 'become' | tikuʔaa ⟨≈tih-koo-'ah⟩ | tiʔaa ⟨≈tih-'ah⟩ | tiʔaariʔ ⟨≈tih-'ah-dih'⟩ | iriiraʔaara ⟨≈ih-dee-duh-'ah-duh⟩ | tiʔaarista ⟨≈tih-'ah-dihs-tuh⟩ | 351 |

| **kaaʔat ⟨kah-'uht⟩** 'go, walk inside, as in a dwelling or room' | tatkaaʔat ⟨≈tuht-kah-'uht⟩ | tikaaʔat ⟨≈tih-kah-'uht⟩ | tikaawuuʔ ⟨≈tih-kah-woo'⟩ | iriirakaata ⟨≈ih-dee-duh-kah-tuh⟩ | tikaahuhta ⟨≈tih-kah-hooh-tuh⟩ | 2 |
| **kuut ⟨koot⟩** 'die' | tikukuut ⟨≈tih-koo-koot⟩ | tikuut ⟨≈tih-koot⟩ | tikuutuʔ ⟨≈tih-koo-too'⟩ | iriirakuuti ⟨≈ih-dee-duh-koo-tih⟩ | tikuutista ⟨≈tih-koo-tihs-tuh⟩ | 452 |

**Blue Book cross-reference**

BB L5 p.52–53: Tiku'ks•taa. My hand has been hurt. (perfect) vs *Tiku'ks•taari'* 'My hand is hurting' (imperfect). BB L15 p.96–97: +u'. Everywhere else, the verb final k will drop off (or, for some — the same fact Parks states for stem-final *k* (GO p.46). ⚠ BB writes the imperfective as *-u'*/*-us*; Parks as *-huʔ*/*-hus*: the *h* is lost after a consonant (Rule 16).

**Practice and answer key**

**Practice C — derive the attested form.**

Given the 3sg perfective of each verb, give the attested 3sg imperfective.

C1. *tikikat ⟨≈tih-kih-kuht⟩* ('cry', stem *kikak ⟨kih-kuh(k/t)⟩*) → ______
C2. *tikiikaʔ ⟨≈tih-kee-kuh'⟩* ('drink', stem *kiikaa ⟨kee-kah⟩*) → ______
C3. *tiwariiʔ ⟨≈tih-wuh-dee'⟩* ('go around, walk around', stem *warii ⟨wuh-dee⟩*) → ______
C4. *tiwiitit ⟨≈tih-wee-tiht⟩* ('sit down, be seated', stem *wiitik ⟨wee-tih(k/t)⟩*) → ______
C5. *tiraa ⟨≈tih-dah⟩* ('have, possess', stem *raah ⟨dah(h)⟩*) → ______

Answers (Parks Part II forms):

C1. *tikikaakuʔ ⟨≈tih-kih-kah-koo'⟩* (Parks p.93, `SK-kikak-p93-1767`)
C2. *tikiikaahuʔ ⟨≈tih-kee-kah-hoo'⟩* (Parks p.90, `SK-kiikaa-p90-1694`)
C3. *tiwariihuʔ ⟨≈tih-wuh-dee-hoo'⟩* (Parks p.177, `SK-warii-p177-3506`)
C4. *tiwiitikuʔ ⟨≈tih-wee-tih-koo'⟩* (Parks p.179, `SK-wiitik-p179-3555`)
C5. *tiraahuʔ ⟨≈tih-dah-hoo'⟩* (Parks p.116, `SK-raah-p116-2264`)

## Lesson 6.2 — the intentive -his + -ta 'is going to'

**Objectives**

- Form the intentive (dictionary form 5) and recognise its shapes *-ista*, *-sta*, *-ksta*.

**New morphemes**

- INT *-ta* after PERF *-his* (→ *-ista*, after *Vk*: *-ksta*; after IMPF *-hus*: *-usta*) — 'is going to ...' — source: Parks GO p.46–47, Table 17; Rule 3R (-his reduction); 2142 dictionary form-5 words; BB L19 p.108

**Construction walkthrough**

Parks GO p.46: *tikíkat* 'he cried' vs *tikikakísta* 'he is going to cry' — the perfective *-his* appears only when *-ta* follows, and after a stem-final *k* it reduces to *-s* (Rule 3R): dictionary *tikikaksta ⟨≈tih-kih-kuhks-tuh⟩*, *tiwiitiksta ⟨≈tih-wee-tihks-tuh⟩*, *tiruuksta ⟨≈tih-dooks-tuh⟩*. After a vowel: *tisahista ⟨≈tih-suh-hihs-tuh⟩*, *tikiikaahista ⟨≈tih-kee-kah-hihs-tuh⟩*, *tiraahista ⟨≈tih-dah-hihs-tuh⟩*, *tiirikista ⟨≈tee-dih-kihs-tuh⟩*. With the imperfective inside: *tiwariihusta ⟨≈tih-wuh-dee-hoos-tuh⟩* (= *warii* + *hus* + *ta*). Descriptive verbs add *-uk* first: *tipahaatuksta ⟨≈tih-puh-hah-tooks-tuh⟩* (Parks GO p.49 cites exactly *tipahaatuksta* 'it is going to be red'). The Blue Book: BB p.108: “by the suffix +ta. It comes as the last part of a verb phrase, and”.

**Dictionary paradigm forms** (Parks Part II of each entry)

| Stem (entry) | 1sg IND (form 1) | 3sg IND (form 2) | IMPF (form 3) | GER (form 4) | INT (form 5) | Parks p. |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **kikak ⟨kih-kuh(k/t)⟩** 'cry' | tatkikat ⟨≈tuht-kih-kuht⟩ | tikikat ⟨≈tih-kih-kuht⟩ | tikikaakuʔ ⟨≈tih-kih-kah-koo'⟩ | iriirakikat ⟨≈ih-dee-duh-kih-kuht⟩ | tikikaksta ⟨≈tih-kih-kuhks-tuh⟩ | 93 |
| **wiitik ⟨wee-tih(k/t)⟩** 'sit down, be seated' | tatpiitit ⟨≈tuht-pee-tiht⟩ | tiwiitit ⟨≈tih-wee-tiht⟩ | tiwiitikuʔ ⟨≈tih-wee-tih-koo'⟩ | iriirawiitit ⟨≈ih-dee-duh-wee-tiht⟩ | tiwiitiksta ⟨≈tih-wee-tihks-tuh⟩ | 179 |
| **raʔuk ⟨duh-'oo(k/t)⟩** 'make' | tahuʔ ⟨≈tuh-hoo'⟩ | tiruʔ ⟨≈tih-doo'⟩ | tiruukuʔ ⟨≈tih-doo-koo'⟩ | iriiraraʔu ⟨≈ih-dee-duh-duh-'oo⟩ | tiruuksta ⟨≈tih-dooks-tuh⟩ | 114 |
| **sa ⟨suh⟩** 'be lying, reclined' | tacta ⟨≈tuhts-tuh⟩ | tiisa ⟨≈tee-suh⟩ | tiisaahuʔ ⟨≈tee-sah-hoo'⟩ | iriiraasa ⟨≈ih-dee-dah-suh⟩ | tisahista ⟨≈tih-suh-hihs-tuh⟩ | 150 |

| **pahaat ⟨puh-haht⟩** 'be red, reddish' | — | tipahaaʔat ⟨≈tih-puh-hah-'uht⟩ | — | iriirapahaatu ⟨≈ih-dee-duh-puh-hah-too⟩ | tipahaatuksta ⟨≈tih-puh-hah-tooks-tuh⟩ | 107 |

**Blue Book cross-reference**

BB L19 p.108 *Tatutari'sta* 'I'll do it' = Parks *tuutarista ⟨≈too-tuh-dihs-tuh⟩*-type build (1sg: *ta* + *t* + *ut* + *aar* + *his* + *ta*); the dictionary gives the 3sg *tuutarista ⟨≈too-tuh-dihs-tuh⟩*.

**Practice and answer key**

**Practice C — derive the attested form.**

Given the 3sg perfective of each verb, give the attested 3sg intentive.

C1. *tikikat ⟨≈tih-kih-kuht⟩* ('cry', stem *kikak ⟨kih-kuh(k/t)⟩*) → ______
C2. *tiisa ⟨≈tee-suh⟩* ('be lying, reclined', stem *sa ⟨suh⟩*) → ______

C2. *tisahista ⟨≈tih-suh-hihs-tuh⟩* (Parks p.150, `SK-sa-p150-2933`)

## Lesson 6.3 — two and many: si-, rak-, raar-, ir-, and the irregular stems of 'go'

**Objectives**

- Read the full indicative paradigm of 'to go' and 'to drink it' (Appendix 1).
- Recognise *si-* (dual), *rak-* (1st/2nd plural), *raar-*/*ir-* (3rd plural) and the suppletive stems *at / war / wuu*.

**New morphemes**

- DU *si-* — dual, before the mode prefix — source: Parks GO p.35; App.1 dual cells (180 forms); BB L4 p.47 'Si tahrarra'
- PL *rak-* (1st/2nd agent) — 'we (excl.), you (pl.)' — source: Parks GO p.35 Table 10; App.1; BB L4 p.47 '+ rak +'
- 1.PL.IN *a-* + *rak-* — 'we (inclusive)' — source: Parks GO p.35 Table 9; App.1 1pl_incl cells
- 1.DU.IN *acir-* — 'you and I' — source: Parks GO p.35 Table 9; App.1 (BB L4 p.47 writes *+ atsir +*)
- 3.PL *ir-* (collective) / *raar-* (individuative) / suffix *-aahuʔ* — 'they' — source: Parks GO p.35–36 Table 10 (tiiraspaatasit / tiiraaraspaatasit); App.1 3pl cells; BB L4 p.47 '+ it + for they'

**Construction walkthrough**

Singular is unmarked (GO p.35). Dual adds *si-* in front of everything: *taatat ⟨≈tah-tuht⟩* 'I went' → *sitatpa ⟨≈sih-tuht-puh⟩* 'we two went' — and the stem of 'go' changes to *war* (surfacing as *pa* after *t*). Plural first/second person adds *rak-*: *tahapuuʔ ⟨≈tuh-huh-poo'⟩*, *tastapuuʔ ⟨≈tuhs-tuh-poo'⟩*, with the plural stem *wuu* (*puuʔ*). Third plural: *tiiwuuʔ, tiraarat ⟨≈tee-woo' / tih-dah-duht⟩*. The regular verb 'drink' shows the prefixes without stem change: *sitatkiikaʔ ⟨≈sih-tuht-kee-kuh'⟩*, *tahakiikaʔ ⟨≈tuh-huh-kee-kuh'⟩*, *tastakiikaʔ ⟨≈tuhs-tuh-kee-kuh'⟩*, *tihkiikaʔ ⟨≈tihh-kee-kuh'⟩*. Appendix 2 lists the suppletive roots (irregular_verb_roots): *at* / *war* / *at~wuu* 'go'; *kus* / *kus* / *wihak* 'be sitting'; *aah* / *aah* / *hurii-waa* 'be around'. BB: BB p.54: “for different subjects. Notice the roots for go are +at for one”.

**Appendix 1 — *to go***

| Person/number | indicative |
|---|---|
| 1sg | taatat ⟨≈tah-tuht⟩ |
| 2sg | taasat ⟨≈tah-suht⟩ |
| 3sg | tiʔat ⟨≈tih-'uht⟩ |
| 1du_incl | tacihwa ⟨≈tuh-chihh-wuh⟩ |

| 1pl_incl | tataraapuuʔ ⟨≈tuh-tuh-dah-poo'⟩ |

| 2pl | tastapuuʔ ⟨≈tuhs-tuh-poo'⟩ |
| 3pl | tiiwuuʔ, tiraarat ⟨≈tee-woo' / tih-dah-duht⟩ |

Source: Parks Appendix 1, *to go*, p.4 (verb_paradigms table).

**Blue Book cross-reference**

BB L4 p.47 verb set 'Who has them?': *Si tahrarra* 'We (2) have them', *Tahraktarra* 'We (3+) have them', *Tihrarra* 'They (3+) have them' (BB analysis *ti + it + rarra*). Parks App.1 'to have it' (class 3) uses *si-* for all non-singular persons: dual *sitahaa ⟨≈sih-tuh-hah⟩*, plural *sitahaa ⟨≈sih-tuh-hah⟩* ⚠ — BB's *Tahraktarra* with *rak-* is not what Appendix 1 shows for this verb; both are given, neither is altered. BB L5 p.54: *Sitat•pa* 'we (2) have gone', *Tahra•pu'* 'we (3+) have gone' = App.1 *sitatpa ⟨≈sih-tuht-puh⟩*, *tahapuuʔ ⟨≈tuh-huh-poo'⟩*.

**Practice and answer key**

**Practice C — derive the attested form.**

C1. 1du_excl → ______
C2. 2du → ______
C3. 3du → ______
C4. 1pl_excl → ______
C5. 2pl → ______
C6. 3pl → ______

Answers (App.1 p.6):

C1. 1du_excl *sitatkiikaʔ ⟨≈sih-tuht-kee-kuh'⟩* 'we drank it'
C2. 2du *sitaskiikaʔ ⟨≈sih-tuhs-kee-kuh'⟩* 'you two drank it'
C3. 3du *sitikiikaʔ ⟨≈sih-tih-kee-kuh'⟩* 'they two drank it'
C4. 1pl_excl *tahakiikaʔ ⟨≈tuh-huh-kee-kuh'⟩* 'we drank it'
C5. 2pl *tastakiikaʔ ⟨≈tuhs-tuh-kee-kuh'⟩* 'you all drank it'
C6. 3pl *tihkiikaʔ ⟨≈tihh-kee-kuh'⟩* 'they drank it'

## Lesson 6.4 — uks-: past time and commands

**Objectives**

- Recognise the aorist/jussive prefix *uks-* in Parks's examples and the Blue Book's past and command forms.

**New morphemes**

- AOR/JUSS *uks-* — optional past tense; with the contingent mode, a command — source: Parks GO p.38 (tatuksat 'I went'; sukskirikaʔaaʔ 'wake up!'); BB L5 p.53, L8 p.66–67, L13 p.89 — ⚠ no dictionary paradigm form contains uks-; Appendix 1 has no aorist mode — attested only in Parks's grammar examples and the Blue Book

**Construction walkthrough**

Parks GO p.38 contrasts *taatat ⟨≈tah-tuht⟩* 'I went' with *tatuksat* 'I went' < *ta-* + *t-* + *uks-* AOR + *at*; the Blue Book builds the same word: BB L13 p.89 *Tatuks•at* 'I went', *Tasu'ks•at* 'You went', *Tuks•at* 'He went'. BB p.66: “the marker +uks, which follows the person marker in the intro-” … (BB L8). With a third person the mode vowel disappears: BB p.67: “The u in +uks is a strong vowel which causes the a in modal”, hence BB *Tuks•kaku'* 'He was in the house' (Parks's rule 1R/6: *ti* + *u...* → *tu...*). Commands: contingent *i-* + *s-* + *uks-* + stem (GO p.38, *sukskirikaʔaaʔ* 'wake up!'); BB L5 p.53 *Suks•paku'* 'Say it!', *Sisuks•paku'* (two hearers), *Suksta•paku'* (three or more; *rak-* → *ta* after *s*).

**Blue Book cross-reference**

BB p.53: “(hearer) marker s +, the command marker +uks, and number” (BB L5). Parks writes the command with the contingent mode prefix *i-* (GO p.38); BB's forms begin directly with *s-* — the *i-* is not written ⚠.

**Practice and answer key**

**Practice C — derive the attested form.**

Break down the Blue Book forms using Parks's slots (mode, person, *uks-*, stem). Answers follow BB's own analyses (L8 p.67; L5 p.53).

C1. *Tasuks•atku* 'You listened' (BB p.66) → ______
C2. *Tuks•kaku'* 'He was in the house' (BB p.67) → ______
C3. *Suks•paku'* 'Say it!' (BB p.53) → ______

Answers: C1 *ta* + *s* + *uks* + *atku* (BB p.66–67); C2 *ti* + Ø + *uks* + *ka* + *ku'* (BB p.67: 'mode person past root root'); C3 *s* + *uks* + *wak* + *u'* (BB p.53: 'you + do it + say it + completed'; *w* → *p* after *s*). These three words are BB-attested; the dictionary has no entries for them.

## Lesson 6.5 — saying no and asking: kaaki-/kaakaa- and ra-

**Objectives**

- Form negative indicatives with *kaakaa-* (1st/2nd) and *kaaki-* (3rd).
- Recognise the absolutive *ra-* used in questions.

**New morphemes**

- NEG.IND *kaakaa-* / *kaaki-* — negative statements — source: Parks GO p.34 Table 8; App.1 negative cells (73 forms); BB L1 p.32 'Kaki', L4 p.46 'Kakah•ra'
- ABS *ra-* — absolutive: questions, narration — source: Parks GO p.34 Table 8; App.1 absolutive cells; BB L4 p.46 'ra + s + ra (that) you have it', L13 p.89 'Take ra'at?'

**Construction walkthrough**

App.1 'to go': *taatat ⟨≈tah-tuht⟩* 'I went' → *kaakaatat ⟨≈kah-kah-tuht⟩* 'I did not go'; *tiʔat ⟨≈tih-'uht⟩* → *kaakiiʔat ⟨≈kah-kee-'uht⟩*. 'Drink': *kaakaatkiikaʔ ⟨≈kah-kaht-kee-kuh'⟩*, *kaakaaskiikaʔ ⟨≈kah-kahs-kee-kuh'⟩*, *kaakiikiikaʔ ⟨≈kah-kee-kee-kuh'⟩*. Absolutive (questions): *raatat ⟨≈dah-tuht⟩*, *raasat ⟨≈dah-suht⟩*, *raʔat ⟨≈duh-'uht⟩*; 'have': *rastaa ⟨≈duhs-tah⟩* — the Blue Book's *Kirike ras•ta?* 'What do you have?' (L4 title).

**Appendix 1 — *to go***

| Person/number | indicative | negative indicative | absolutive |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1sg | taatat ⟨≈tah-tuht⟩ | kaakaatat ⟨≈kah-kah-tuht⟩ | raatat ⟨≈dah-tuht⟩ |
| 2sg | taasat ⟨≈tah-suht⟩ | kaakaasat ⟨≈kah-kah-suht⟩ | raasat ⟨≈dah-suht⟩ |
| 3sg | tiʔat ⟨≈tih-'uht⟩ | kaakiiʔat ⟨≈kah-kee-'uht⟩ | raʔat ⟨≈duh-'uht⟩ |
| 3pl | tiiwuuʔ, tiraarat ⟨≈tee-woo' / tih-dah-duht⟩ | kaakiiwuuʔ, kaakiraarat ⟨≈kah-kee-woo' / kah-kih-dah-duht⟩ | raawuuʔ, raraarat ⟨≈dah-woo' / duh-dah-duht⟩ |

Source: Parks Appendix 1, *to go*, p.4 (verb_paradigms table).

**Appendix 1 — *to drink it***

| Person/number | negative indicative | absolutive |
|---|---|---|
| 1sg | kaakaatkiikaʔ ⟨≈kah-kaht-kee-kuh'⟩ | ratkiikaʔ ⟨≈duht-kee-kuh'⟩ |
| 2sg | kaakaaskiikaʔ ⟨≈kah-kahs-kee-kuh'⟩ | raskiikaʔ ⟨≈duhs-kee-kuh'⟩ |
| 3sg | kaakiikiikaʔ ⟨≈kah-kee-kee-kuh'⟩ | rakiikaʔ ⟨≈duh-kee-kuh'⟩ |

Source: Parks Appendix 1, *to drink it*, p.6 (verb_paradigms table).

**Blue Book cross-reference**

BB L1 p.32 *Kaki kisu'* 'It is not a bone'; BB L4 p.46 *Kakah•ra* 'I don't have it' vs Parks App.1 *kaakahaa ⟨≈kah-kuh-hah⟩* ⚠ (BB keeps *r*, Parks has *haa*; same difference as *tahra*/*tahaa* in Lesson 5.2).

**Practice and answer key**

**Practice C — derive the attested form.**

Appendix 1, *to go*, negative indicative perfective: given 1sg *kaakaatat ⟨≈kah-kah-tuht⟩*, fill the other cells.

C1. 2sg → ______
C2. 3sg → ______
C3. 1du_excl → ______
C4. 1pl_excl → ______
C5. 3pl → ______

Answers (App.1 p.4):

C1. 2sg *kaakaasat ⟨≈kah-kah-suht⟩* 'you did not go'
C2. 3sg *kaakiiʔat ⟨≈kah-kee-'uht⟩* 'he did not go'
C3. 1du_excl *sikaakatpa ⟨≈sih-kah-kuht-puh⟩* 'we did not go'
C4. 1pl_excl *kaakaahapuuʔ ⟨≈kah-kah-huh-poo'⟩* 'we did not go'
C5. 3pl *kaakiiwuuʔ, kaakiraarat ⟨≈kah-kee-woo' / kah-kih-dah-duht⟩* 'they did not go'

Everything below is an attested dictionary word made only of morphemes taught so far (Units 1–6). Break each down, gloss the pieces, and name any sound change at the seams; then rebuild it from the pieces.

| Word | Class | Meaning (Parks) | Parks analysis | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **kickita ⟨kihts-kih-tuh⟩** | VL | be a branch, fork in a stream, as a creek or river | kic 'liquid' + kita 'be a fork in' | Parks p.87, `SK-kickita-p87-1636` |
| **kickus ⟨kihts-koo(s)⟩** | VI (4) | be a body of water, as a pool or puddle | kic 'water' + kus 'to be sitting' | Parks p.87, `SK-kickus-p87-1640` |
| **kickuut ⟨kihts-koot⟩** | VP (2) | sour, spoil, rot, as a liquid, fruit, vegetable | kic 'liquid' + kuut 'to die/ spoil' | Parks p.87, `SK-kickuut-p87-1642` |
| **kiictariiʔuus ⟨KEETS-tuh-dee-'oos⟩** | N | mallard (Anas platyrhynchos) | kiic 'neck' + tariiʔuus 'blue' | Parks p.89, `SK-kiictariiquus-p89-1670` |
| **kitaʔat ⟨kih-tuh-'uht⟩** | VI (1) | go, walk on top | kita 'on top' + at 'go' | Parks p.98, `SK-kitaqat-p98-1907` |
| **kitawuh ⟨kih-tuh-woo(h)⟩** | VT (1) | count | kita 'on top' + wuh 'put' | Parks p.99, `SK-kitawuh-p99-1919` |
| **pahaatkaatiit ⟨puh-haht-kah-teet⟩** | VD | be deep red, maroon, wine, oxblood | pahaat 'red' + kaatiit 'dark' | Parks p.107, `SK-pahaatkaatiit-p107-2051` |
| **pakcak ⟨puhk-chuh(k/t)⟩** | VT (4) | shoot, hit, strike, as with a bullet or an arrow | paks 'head' + cak 'strikes' | Parks p.108, `SK-pakcak-p108-2076` |
| **pakskaatiit ⟨PUHKS-kah-teet⟩** | N | Frenchman | paks 'head' + kaatiit 'black' | Parks p.109, `SK-pakskaatiit-p109-2091` |
| **pakskatiit ⟨puhks-kuh-teet⟩** | VD | be black haired, have black hair | paks 'head' + katiit 'be black' | Parks p.109, `SK-pakskatiit-p109-2092` |

Answer key (Parks's analysis, sound rules fired, confidence):

- *kickita ⟨kihts-kih-tuh⟩* = kic 'liquid' + kita 'be a fork in' — no change at the seams — high (Parks p.87, `SK-kickita-p87-1636`)
- *kickus ⟨kihts-koo(s)⟩* = kic 'water' + kus 'to be sitting' — no change at the seams — high (Parks p.87, `SK-kickus-p87-1640`)
- *kickuut ⟨kihts-koot⟩* = kic 'liquid' + kuut 'to die/ spoil' — no change at the seams — high (Parks p.87, `SK-kickuut-p87-1642`)
- *kiictariiʔuus ⟨KEETS-tuh-dee-'oos⟩* = kiic 'neck' + tariiʔuus 'blue' — no change at the seams — high (Parks p.89, `SK-kiictariiquus-p89-1670`)
- *kitaʔat ⟨kih-tuh-'uht⟩* = kita 'on top' + at 'go' — rules: ʔ-epenthesis (V+V) — high (Parks p.98, `SK-kitaqat-p98-1907`)
- *kitawuh ⟨kih-tuh-woo(h)⟩* = kita 'on top' + wuh 'put' — no change at the seams — high (Parks p.99, `SK-kitawuh-p99-1919`)
- *pahaatkaatiit ⟨puh-haht-kah-teet⟩* = pahaat 'red' + kaatiit 'dark' — no change at the seams — high (Parks p.107, `SK-pahaatkaatiit-p107-2051`)
- *pakcak ⟨puhk-chuh(k/t)⟩* = paks 'head' + cak 'strikes' — rules: 9R — high (Parks p.108, `SK-pakcak-p108-2076`)
- *pakskaatiit ⟨PUHKS-kah-teet⟩* = paks 'head' + kaatiit 'black' — no change at the seams — high (Parks p.109, `SK-pakskaatiit-p109-2091`)
- *pakskatiit ⟨puhks-kuh-teet⟩* = paks 'head' + katiit 'be black' — no change at the seams — high (Parks p.109, `SK-pakskatiit-p109-2092`)

# Unit 7 — Subordinate forms, reflexives, derivation, descriptive verbs

The remaining pieces needed to read any dictionary entry: the gerundial (form 4) with its class-dependent ending, the reflexive proclitic, the causative and inchoative suffixes, and the descriptive-verb existential *-uk*.

**Blue Book alignment.** BB L13 (making verb phrases into nouns), L14 (reflexive marker), L2/L3 (descriptive 'ti pahaat', 'ti rihu').

## Lesson 7.1 — irii- ra- ... : the gerundial and the verb classes

**Objectives**

- Read dictionary form 4 as *irii-* 'the one' + *ra-* ABS + stem + subordinating suffix.
- Use the ending to identify Parks's verb class: class 1 *-a*, class 2 *-i*, class 3 final-syllable change, class 4 Ø, descriptive *-u*, locative *-wi*.

**New morphemes**

- ABS/GER *ra-* — subordinate mode: 'the one who..., when...' — source: Parks GO p.34 Table 8; 2190 dictionary form-4 words
- *irii-* 'the one; what, where' — deictic proclitic opening the dictionary's gerundial forms — source: Parks GO p.42 (irii- 'what, where'; iriiʔahaarasika 'where he cooked'); BB L13 p.88 'hiri'
- SUB suffixes by class: 1 *-a*, 2 *-i*, 3 final-syllable change, 4 Ø, D *-u*, L *-wi* — subordinate perfective marker — source: Parks GO p.46 Table 17; p.50 (-wi); morpheme_inventory slot 28

**Construction walkthrough**

Class 1 (*-a*): *at ⟨uht⟩* → *iriiraata ⟨≈ih-dee-dah-tuh⟩*; *kaaʔat ⟨kah-'uht⟩* → *iriirakaata ⟨≈ih-dee-duh-kah-tuh⟩*; *raah ⟨dah(h)⟩* → *iriiraraaha ⟨≈ih-dee-duh-dah-huh⟩*. Class 4 (Ø): *wiitik ⟨wee-tih(k/t)⟩* → *iriirawiitit ⟨≈ih-dee-duh-wee-tiht⟩*, *kikak ⟨kih-kuh(k/t)⟩* → *iriirakikat ⟨≈ih-dee-duh-kih-kuht⟩*, *kus ⟨koo(s)⟩* → *iriiraaku ⟨≈ih-dee-dah-koo⟩*, *sa ⟨suh⟩* → *iriiraasa ⟨≈ih-dee-dah-suh⟩*. Class 2-i (*-i*): *arik ⟨uh-dih(k/t)⟩* → *iriiraariki ⟨≈ih-dee-dah-dih-kih⟩*, *kuut ⟨koot⟩* → *iriirakuuti ⟨≈ih-dee-duh-koo-tih⟩*. Class 3 (final syllable changes): *raʔuk ⟨duh-'oo(k/t)⟩* → *iriiraraʔu ⟨≈ih-dee-duh-duh-'oo⟩*. Class 1-i: *aar ⟨ah(d)⟩* → *iriiraʔaara ⟨≈ih-dee-duh-'ah-duh⟩*. Descriptive (*-u*): *pahaat ⟨puh-haht⟩* → *iriirapahaatu ⟨≈ih-dee-duh-puh-hah-too⟩* (Parks GO p.49 cites *iriirapahaattu* 'the one that is red'). Locative (*-wi*): *kaa ⟨kah⟩* → *iriirakaawi ⟨≈ih-dee-duh-kah-wih⟩*, *ikskaa ⟨ihks-kah⟩* → *iriiraakskaawi ⟨≈ih-dee-dahks-kah-wih⟩*. App.1 gerundials: *iriiraata ⟨≈ih-dee-dah-tuh⟩*, *iriirakikaa ⟨≈ih-dee-duh-kih-kah⟩*, *iriruuhiira ⟨≈ih-dih-doo-hee-duh⟩*.

**Dictionary paradigm forms** (Parks Part II of each entry)

| Stem (entry) | 1sg IND (form 1) | 3sg IND (form 2) | IMPF (form 3) | GER (form 4) | INT (form 5) | Parks p. |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **at ⟨uht⟩** 'go' | taatat ⟨≈tah-tuht⟩ | tiʔat ⟨≈tih-'uht⟩ | tiwuuʔ ⟨≈tih-woo'⟩ | iriiraata ⟨≈ih-dee-dah-tuh⟩ | tihuhta ⟨≈tih-hooh-tuh⟩ | 18 |
| **kaaʔat ⟨kah-'uht⟩** 'go, walk inside, as in a dwelling or room' | tatkaaʔat ⟨≈tuht-kah-'uht⟩ | tikaaʔat ⟨≈tih-kah-'uht⟩ | tikaawuuʔ ⟨≈tih-kah-woo'⟩ | iriirakaata ⟨≈ih-dee-duh-kah-tuh⟩ | tikaahuhta ⟨≈tih-kah-hooh-tuh⟩ | 2 |
| **raah ⟨dah(h)⟩** 'have, possess' | tahaa ⟨≈tuh-hah⟩ | tiraa ⟨≈tih-dah⟩ | tiraahuʔ ⟨≈tih-dah-hoo'⟩ | iriiraraaha ⟨≈ih-dee-duh-dah-huh⟩ | tiraahista ⟨≈tih-dah-hihs-tuh⟩ | 116 |
| **wiitik ⟨wee-tih(k/t)⟩** 'sit down, be seated' | tatpiitit ⟨≈tuht-pee-tiht⟩ | tiwiitit ⟨≈tih-wee-tiht⟩ | tiwiitikuʔ ⟨≈tih-wee-tih-koo'⟩ | iriirawiitit ⟨≈ih-dee-duh-wee-tiht⟩ | tiwiitiksta ⟨≈tih-wee-tihks-tuh⟩ | 179 |

## Lesson 7.2 — witi- : doing it to oneself

**Objectives**

- Recognise the reflexive/reciprocal proclitic *witi-* in dictionary VR forms and in BB L14.

**New morphemes**

- REFL *witi-* — reflexive / reciprocal: subject = object — source: Parks GO p.40 (wititaatacikstariku 'I keep my thoughts to myself'); dictionary VR forms; BB L14 p.93 'Witi tatut•erit' 'I saw myself'

**Construction walkthrough**

Reflexive verbs (VR) carry *witi-* before the mode prefix in every dictionary form: *acikstaʔuk ⟨uh-chihks-tuh-'oo(k/t)⟩* 'decide, make up one's mind' → 3sg *wititiicikstuʔ ⟨≈wih-tih-tee-chihks-too'⟩*, 1sg *wititaatacikstuʔ ⟨≈wih-tih-tah-tuh-chihks-too'⟩*; *acikstiihac ⟨uh-chihks-tee-huhts⟩* → *wititiicikstiihac ⟨≈wih-tih-tee-chihks-tee-huhts⟩*. Verbs that are both transitive and reflexive list both forms: *askawat ⟨uhs-kuh-wuht⟩* 'take a moccasin off' 3sg *tiiskawat; wititiiskawat ⟨≈tees-kuh-wuht / wih-tih-tees-kuh-wuht⟩*; *asuuktik ⟨uh-sook-tih(k/t)⟩* 'put footwear on, put moccasins on' 3sg *tiisuuktit; wititiisuuktit ⟨≈tee-sook-tiht / wih-tih-tee-sook-tiht⟩*. BB p.92: “Witi tatut•erit.” (BB L14).

**Dictionary paradigm forms** (Parks Part II of each entry)

| **acikstiihac ⟨uh-chihks-tee-huhts⟩** 'decide, make up one's mind' | wititaatacikstiihac ⟨≈wih-tih-tah-tuh-chihks-tee-huhts⟩ | wititiicikstiihac ⟨≈wih-tih-tee-chihks-tee-huhts⟩ | wititiicikstiihaacuʔ ⟨≈wih-tih-tee-chihks-tee-hah-choo'⟩ | iriiwitiraacikstiihac ⟨≈ih-dee-wih-tih-dah-chihks-tee-huhts⟩ | wititiicikstiihacista ⟨≈wih-tih-tee-chihks-tee-huh-chihs-tuh⟩ | 7 |

## Lesson 7.3 — making new verbs: -raʔuk / -ik / -rik / -wuh (cause), -aar / -itik (become), -waa (distributive)

**Objectives**

- Derive causatives and inchoatives from known roots with Parks's stem-template suffixes (GO p.44 Table 15; p.50).

**New morphemes**

- CAUS *-raʔuk* — 'cause to be, make' — source: Parks GO p.44 Table 15 slot 8; 102 etymologies
- CAUS *-ik*, *-rik*, *-k*, *-wuh* — 'cause to; put' — source: Parks GO p.44 Table 15; 40 / 29 / 28 / 48 etymologies
- INCH *-aar* 'become', *-itik* — inchoative 'become, get; begin' — source: Parks GO p.48, p.50 (table of -aar forms); 56 / 29 etymologies
- DIST *-waa* — distributive 'here and there, several' — source: Parks abbreviations (dist.); 87 etymologies

**Construction walkthrough**

*kaatiit* 'black' + *raʔuk* → *katiihaʔuk ⟨kuh-tee-huh-'oo(k/t)⟩* 'blacken, make black, color black': *t* + *r* → *hr* → *h* (Rules 13–15), 3sg *tikatiihuʔ ⟨≈tih-kuh-tee-hoo'⟩*. *icka* 'face, point' + *raʔuk* → *ickaraʔuk ⟨ihts-kuh-duh-'oo(k/t)⟩* 'sharpen, sharpen to a point'; *akaar* + *raʔuk* → *akaraʔuk ⟨uh-kuh-duh-'oo(k/t)⟩* 'build, construct a dwelling, as an earthlodge, tipi, house' (*rr* → *r*, Rule 20). *kaas* 'leg; break' + *ik* → *kaasik ⟨kah-sih(k/t)⟩* 'break, crack, as a nut shell'; *hiir* (uur...) + *ik* → *hiirik ⟨hee-dih(k/t)⟩* 'fix, fix nicely, as something one works on'. *kiikaa* + *rik* → *kikaarik ⟨kih-kah-dih(k/t)⟩* 'water, give water to, as an animal'. *kiic* + *kaa* + *wuh* → *kiickaawuh ⟨keets-kah-woo(h)⟩* 'put around someone's neck'. Inchoatives: *kic* + *aar* → *kicaar ⟨kih-chah(d)⟩* 'become watery' (3sg *tikicaa ⟨≈tih-kih-chah⟩*); *kirikaʔaar ⟨kih-dih-kuh-'ah(d)⟩* 'wake up, awaken'; *kituuʔaar ⟨kih-too-'ah(d)⟩*; *raahiiriitik ⟨dah-hee-dee-tih(k/t)⟩* 'improve in health, get well, recover, as someone who has been ill'. Distributive: *raahiwaar ⟨dah-hih-wah(d)⟩* 'be dressed up' = *raa* + *hiir* + *waa*; *uuspaa ⟨oos-pah⟩* = *uus* + *waa* (*w* → *p* after *s*).

**Attested example words**

|---|---|---|---|---|
| **katiihaʔuk ⟨kuh-tee-huh-'oo(k/t)⟩** | VT (3) | blacken, make black, color black | katiit 'black' + raʔuk 'CAUS -raʔuk' | Parks p.82, `SK-katiihaquk-p82-1530` |
| **ickaraʔuk ⟨ihts-kuh-duh-'oo(k/t)⟩** | VT (3) | sharpen, sharpen to a point | icka 'sharp' + raʔuk 'CAUS -raʔuk' | Parks p.57, `SK-ickaraquk-p57-0981` |

**Practice and answer key**

**Practice A — build the word.** Combine the pieces (Parks's own analysis) into the attested word. Watch for the sound changes listed in the walkthrough.

1. *katiit* 'black' + *raʔuk* 'CAUS -raʔuk'  → ______ ('blacken, make black, color black')
2. *akaar* 'dwelling' + *raʔuk* 'CAUS -raʔuk'  → ______ ('build, construct a dwelling, as an earthlodge, tipi, house')
3. *kaas* 'break' + *ik* 'CAUS -ik'  → ______ ('break, crack, as a nut shell')
4. *kic* 'liquid' + *aar* 'become'  → ______ ('become watery')
5. *uus* 'hair' + *waa* 'DIST -waa'  → ______ ('be growing hair')

**Practice B — break down the word.** Divide each attested word into its morphemes and gloss each.

1. *ickaraʔuk ⟨ihts-kuh-duh-'oo(k/t)⟩* 'sharpen, sharpen to a point'  → ______
2. *kikaarik ⟨kih-kah-dih(k/t)⟩* 'water, give water to, as an animal'  → ______
3. *kiickaawuh ⟨keets-kah-woo(h)⟩* 'put around someone's neck'  → ______
4. *kirikaʔaar ⟨kih-dih-kuh-'ah(d)⟩* 'wake up, awaken'  → ______
5. *raahiwaar ⟨dah-hih-wah(d)⟩* 'be dressed up'  → ______

**Answer key (A and B)**

A1. *katiihaʔuk ⟨kuh-tee-huh-'oo(k/t)⟩* (Parks p.82, `SK-katiihaquk-p82-1530`) — rules at the seams: 13, 15 — segmentation confidence high
A2. *akaraʔuk ⟨uh-kuh-duh-'oo(k/t)⟩* (Parks p.11, `SK-akaraquk-p11-0110`) — rules at the seams: 20, tolerance: vowel length/ʔ/h — segmentation confidence medium
A3. *kaasik ⟨kah-sih(k/t)⟩* (Parks p.73, `SK-kaasik-p73-1315`) — segmentation confidence high
A4. *kicaar ⟨kih-chah(d)⟩* (Parks p.84, `SK-kicaar-p84-1573`) — segmentation confidence high

## Lesson 7.4 — descriptive verbs: the existential -uk and vowel reduplication

**Objectives**

- Read the 3sg of colour and quality roots as stem + *-uk* 'be' (null word-finally) with the final-syllable vowel repeated (Parks GO p.49).
- Form their intentive (*-uksta*) and gerundial (*-u*).

**New morphemes**

- EXIST *-uk* 'be, exist' (Ø word-finally; *-u* subordinate; *-uksta* intentive) — turns a descriptive stem into a predicate — source: Parks GO p.49–50 (tipahaaʔat; iriirapahaattu; tipahaatuksta)
- Rule 4R (stem-final vocalic reduplication) — V(V) → V(V)ʔV between consonants in non-subordinate descriptive forms — source: Parks Ch. 3 Rule 4R; GO p.49 'Class I descriptive verbs'

**Construction walkthrough**

Parks GO p.49: *tipahaaʔat* 'it is red' < *ti-* + *pahaat* + *-uk*. Dictionary 3sg forms: *tipahaaʔat ⟨≈tih-puh-hah-'uht⟩*, *tikaatiiʔit ⟨≈tih-kah-tee-'iht⟩*, *tikiiskaaʔac ⟨≈tih-kees-kah-'uhts⟩* 'be pure white', *tikiʔic ⟨≈tih-kih-'ihts⟩* 'be a liquid', *tihaaʔas ⟨≈tih-hah-'uhs⟩* — Class I, with the reduplicated vowel. Class II (no reduplication): *titariiʔuus ⟨≈tih-tuh-dee-'oos⟩*, *tikatuus ⟨≈tih-kuh-toos⟩*, *tikiiʔac ⟨≈tih-kee-'uhts⟩*. Stems in *r* lose it: *titaakaa ⟨≈tih-tah-kah⟩*, *tihuraa ⟨≈tih-hoo-dah⟩*, *tikaahuraa ⟨≈tih-kah-hoo-dah⟩*, *tihii ⟨≈tih-hee⟩* 'it is grassy'. Intentive: *tipahaatuksta ⟨≈tih-puh-hah-tooks-tuh⟩*, *titaakarista ⟨≈tih-tah-kuh-dihs-tuh⟩*; gerundial: *iriirapahaatu ⟨≈ih-dee-duh-puh-hah-too⟩*, *iriirakaatiitu ⟨≈ih-dee-duh-kah-tee-too⟩*, *iriirakiicu ⟨≈ih-dee-duh-kee-choo⟩*.

**Blue Book cross-reference**

BB L2 p.33 *ti pahaat*, L3 p.41 *ti rihu* 'it's big', *ti ha"k•tihu* 'it's big wood' (OCR). ⚠ BB writes descriptive 3sg without the reduplicated *-ʔa-*; Parks: *tipahaaʔat ⟨≈tih-puh-hah-'uht⟩*. For *rihuur* the dictionary gives no form 2 (gap); compounds show it: *tihaakarihuuʔ ⟨≈tih-hah-kuh-dih-hoo'⟩* 'have a large mouth, as a person'.

**Practice and answer key**

**Practice C — derive the attested form.**

Given the 3sg existential of each verb, give the attested 3sg intentive.

C1. *tikaatiiʔit ⟨≈tih-kah-tee-'iht⟩* ('be black', stem *kaatiit ⟨kah-teet⟩*) → ______
C2. *titaakaa ⟨≈tih-tah-kah⟩* ('be white', stem *taakaar ⟨tah-kah(d)⟩*) → ______
C3. *tikiʔic ⟨≈tih-kih-'ihts⟩* ('be a liquid', stem *kic ⟨kihts⟩*) → ______
C4. *tihaaʔas ⟨≈tih-hah-'uhs⟩* ('be string, be a string', stem *haas ⟨hahs⟩*) → ______
C5. *tikatuus ⟨≈tih-kuh-toos⟩* ('be flat, as something naturally flat', stem *katuus ⟨kuh-toos⟩*) → ______

Answers (Parks Part II forms):

C1. *tikaatiituksta ⟨≈tih-kah-tee-tooks-tuh⟩* (Parks p.74, `SK-kaatiit-p74-1330`)
C2. *titaakarista ⟨≈tih-tah-kuh-dihs-tuh⟩* (Parks p.502, `SK-taakaar-p502-4233`)
C3. *tikicuksta ⟨≈tih-kih-chooks-tuh⟩* (Parks p.84, `SK-kic-p84-1568`)
C4. *tihaasuksta ⟨≈tih-hah-sooks-tuh⟩* (Parks p.36, `SK-haas-p36-0613`)
C5. *tikatuusuksta ⟨≈tih-kuh-too-sooks-tuh⟩* (Parks p.83, `SK-katuus-p83-1540`)
